Hit Your Mark Travel · Implementation plan for review

A real destination for every experience tile

Where each of the 126 tiles on the 12 experience pages should link, which existing pages carry that weight, the few new pages worth building, and the order to ship it in. Planning only: nothing on the site has changed.

Prepared 3 Oct 2026Source: main @ 5f547a7Production compared 2 Oct 2026Branch: plan/tile-detail-pages

Labels used throughout: Verified read from source, dist or production · Recommendation a decision this plan proposes · Uncertain needs a fact or a call before it can be acted on.

01Executive recommendation

126tiles audited: 78 sub-experience cards, 48 "Trips We Plan Often" cards
126link to /plan-your-trip/ today, in source, dist and production
62of 78 cards resolve to a page that already exists
9new pages recommended, plus 5 that depend on owner decisions

Recommendation Link the 78 sub-experience cards to existing pages first, and build new pages only where nothing on the site answers the click. 62 of the 78 resolve to an existing destination page, hub or journal post, with no new page needed. 25 of those cards need a targeted enhancement (one FAQ, one itinerary card, a corrected season), not a new section. Keep the 48 itinerary cards' "Plan This Trip" buttons on the form, add a ?type= pre-fill where the form supports one, and add at most one "in detail →" text link per card.

Build 9 new pages: Orlando (Disney World and Universal), St. Lucia and the Dolomites as destination pages, and six nested experience-detail pages: Tennis, Formula 1 and Golf under Sports & Event Travel, and Private Yacht Charters, European River Cruises and Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises under Cruises. Five more tiles (Namibia, Disneyland, interior Mexico, the American Southwest, Football & Rugby) wait on whether HYMT actually sells those trips.

Ship it through one tile map (src/data/tiles.mjs) that both the build and the destination pages read. The map rewrites the card links and generates a "Featured in" strip back to each experience page. Four new verifier checks keep a card from ever pointing back at the form by accident.

Why this shape

  • Every tile gets a real page, but no new URL competes with an existing one. The page audits proposed 12 new URLs and floated more (Croatia, Turkey, a horse-racing page, per-event pages, an Alps ski page). Consolidation kept 9, gated 4 on owner decisions and rejected the rest; §10 lists each with its reason.
  • The reuse work is small and safe. It changes hrefs and adds short modules on pages that already rank for the subject, with word budgets checked against the 3,500-word ceiling.
  • New pages go only where a visitor's question has no answer on the site today: which Orlando park, a St. Lucia-only week, a Dolomites hut trek, how a Wimbledon debenture works, what a crewed charter costs.

How this plan was built

12 parallel page audits (one per experience page, each reading the page source and every candidate target's source), one independent architecture read of the layouts, schema and verifier, and one site-wide consolidation pass that collapsed the 126 tile rows into 56 subjects with one canonical URL each. Every decision except the 22 high-confidence unchanged reuses then went through two adversarial reviewers, one on duplication and SEO and one on whether the click delivers what the tile promises. 0 of 34 decisions were refuted; 28 were upheld with changes, and those changes are applied throughout this plan. Each of the 13 new or conditional pages then got a full content brief.

02Current-state architecture

Verified against HEAD 5f547a7 and production on 2 Oct 2026.

How the pages are assembled

  • Every /experiences/<slug>/ page is two files: src/pages/experiences/<slug>/index.astro (title, description, name, hero props) wrapping src/content-pages/experiences__<slug>.html, imported ?raw. ExperienceLayout.astro owns a hardcoded three-crumb trail (Home › Experiences › Name), the hero, and the JSON-LD: BreadcrumbList, WebPage, Service, and FAQPage parsed from the body.
  • Every /destinations/<slug>/ page has the same shape on DestinationLayout.astro, plus a region prop that adds the fourth crumb on the 59 country pages; the 9 regional hubs omit it. Schema is BreadcrumbList, WebPage, TouristDestination and FAQPage. The hero stat rail must carry "Best Season" and "Best For".
  • The /experiences/ and /destinations/ hubs are hand-written card HTML on Base.astro, with a CollectionPage ItemList built from a depth-1 glob. A nested route such as /experiences/cruises/yacht-charters/ joins neither ItemList and does not change the "12 trip types" count that public/llms.txt states and the verifier enforces.
  • Canonical, og:url and every JSON-LD URL derive from astro.config.mjs → site, and canonicals drop the query string, so /plan-your-trip/?type=safari is invisible to search.

The tiles today

  • 78 .exp-card anchors in an .exp-cards--photo grid under section#destinations: 6 per page, 9 on Family and Food & Wine. The card is the link, so nothing inside it may be a link (verifier nested-card-anchor).
  • 48 .event-card "Trips We Plan Often" cards (4 per page), each with an a.event-cta "Plan This Trip" button. The card itself is a div.
  • All 126 point at /plan-your-trip/. There is no tile map; targets are 126 literal hrefs. The "Explore →" arrow on every card promises a page it does not deliver.
  • Same pattern elsewhere, out of scope here: 330 of 386 .place-cards on destination pages and 248 .itin-cta buttons also point at the form. The data model below is sized so the same mechanism can serve them later.

Reverse links

Only 3 of 68 destination pages link to any experience page (Costa Rica, Dominican Republic, Zambia & Victoria Falls). Each destination page has one stable anchor, id="places". Maldives has id="experiences" instead and a different section set. Neither id="itineraries" nor any per-section id exists yet.

Existing data patterns to copy

tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs plus tools/p3-8-cost-insert.mjs (a keyed data file and an idempotent script that rewrites a marked section) is the established pattern for data-driven content without a component refactor. tools/p3-6-related-destinations.mjs is a second example.

Build gates that constrain this work

npm run build runs 63 named verifier checks. The ones this project touches: internal-links (every same-site href must resolve, though it strips fragments), nested-card-anchor, photo-grid, internal-link-floor (≥2 journal links per destination or experience page), page-length (3,500 body words on 4-crumb destination pages), head-baseline, schema-required, sitemap-parity, llms-txt counts, and the image attribute and ratio checks. Rebuilding an unchanged tree leaves dist/ byte-identical, so the regression test for any layout change is an empty git diff --stat dist/.

Production vs source

Production matches source for everything this plan touches (the sitemap's 122 URLs are identical). The only drift is documentary: README.md still says "43 destination pages" and that production is not on this host. That is a docs chore outside this project.

03Key findings and risks

Verified Findings

  1. Every tile on every experience page dead-ends at the form. All 126, confirmed in source, dist and production. The "Explore →" arrow is a broken promise on 78 cards.
  2. Most tiles already have a strong page. 41 subjects (62 of the 78 cards) map to an existing page with fit 2–3 out of 3: the page is about the subject, or a full section covers it.
  3. Fragment links are not checked today. The dead-link check strips #fragment, so /destinations/maldives/#places would ship green although Maldives has no such id. The plan adds a fragment check before any deep link ships.
  4. Word ceilings are tight. 27 country pages have under 200 words of headroom. The five tightest have 62–68: Arctic Norway, Israel, Colombia, Dominican Republic and UAE & Gulf. Any reverse link on a tight page is wrapped on existing words, and any layout-rendered module must be excluded from bodyWords.
  5. Tile copy contradicts its target page in several places. Family Safari calls Sabi Sand "malaria-free", against the CDC-cited South Africa page. Other examples: the Seychelles, St. Barth's, Sri Lanka and Bali seasons; the Peru acclimatization order; the Sunrise Seto train (it terminates at Takamatsu, not Matsuyama). Linking a tile one click from its contradiction makes this worse, so these copy fixes ship before the links.
  6. Hub card tags disagree with the tiles on 10 of 12 experience hub cards. Examples: the Wellness card names Switzerland and Thailand, which have no tile; the Adventure card names Verbier and the Coral Triangle, which no page covers.
  7. Thin reverse linking. 3 of 68 destination pages link to any experience page, and Family and Multigenerational sell the same Italy and Hawaii trips with no link between them.
  8. The form's ?type= pre-fill works for exactly six values: beach, cruise, family, group, honeymoon, safari (plan-your-trip.html line 358, kebab-case validated).

Risks

RiskLevelMitigation
DestinationLayout.astro re-renders all 68 pages, and 3 verifier predicates plus 4 real-dist fixtures read its markup.High"Featured in" renders nothing when a page has no tile records, so dist stays byte-identical until a record names a page. bodyWords strips .featured-in, keeping the italy=3235 fixture. Run GitNexus impact() first.
ExperienceLayout.astro re-renders 12 pages; breadcrumb and BreadcrumbList share one trail array.MediumOptional parent prop defaulting to today's trail; prove with an empty dist diff.
Deep links to ids that do not exist pass the build.Highcard-fragment-resolves check, with red fixtures, in the same PR as the first fragment href.
Enhancements push a country page over 3,500 words.MediumPer-page word budget in §7; re-measure with the verifier's bodyWords() after every edit; stop at ~3,400.
New pages ship with invented first-hand claims.MediumEvery brief lists its NEEDS MARK items, and no page ships with one unresolved.
Concurrent PRs conflict on MANIFEST.json, head-baseline.json, the sitemap and llms.txt counts.LowOne new page per PR; merge origin/main first; bump the destination count relative to main at merge time.
A merge to main deploys production.MediumTreat each phase PR as a release; run npm run verify:prod about 10 minutes after merge.

04Tile-to-page matrix

One row per tile, all 126. Click a row for the full record: visitor and search intent, existing match and fit, reason, content gaps, link changes, breadcrumb, schema, cannibalization risk, images, dependencies and the subject it belongs to. Changes from the adversarial review are already applied, and each row's reason starts with them.

IDExperienceTileActionRecommended targetPriEffortConf.

IDs: experience code + T (sub-experience card) or E (Trips We Plan Often card) + position on the page. For an E row, "Form CTA" means the button stays on /plan-your-trip/ with the pre-fill from §10, and the URL after it is the one optional secondary text link.

05Routing rules

Recommendation Apply these in order; the first rule that fits decides. Every rule names one canonical URL. No sub-experience card links to /plan-your-trip/ once its target exists, nor to a query-string variant, nor to a page that exists only to receive the tile.

  1. R1 · Exact destination match. The tile names a place with its own page, and that page's intro, places grid, itineraries or FAQ serves the tile at fit ≥2 → REUSE. Maldives → /destinations/maldives/.
  2. R2 · One destination under several experiences. One canonical URL regardless of how many experience pages feature it. The tile copy carries the intent; the destination carries one "Featured in" strip and, where the audit found a real gap, one targeted FAQ or card, never a section per experience. Japan serves 5 tiles on 5 pages.
  3. R3 · Combined tiles ("A & B"). (a) One established product covered by one page → reuse and keep the label: Bora Bora & French Polynesia. (b) One half is a modifier → reuse the subject's page and rename the tile if the modifier over-promises: "Jordan & the Levant" → Jordan. (c) Two separately booked places with coverage for only one → link the stronger half and drop or add the other: "St. Lucia & Anguilla" → St. Lucia. A combination page only when the pairing is sold as one trip and nothing covers either half. None qualified.
  4. R4 · Regional tile with a hub. If the intent is "pick somewhere in this region", link the hub: Caribbean → /destinations/caribbean-mexico/#places; Antarctica & the Arctic → /destinations/polar-regions/. If the tile is a format (river cruising), the hub is the wrong answer; see R5.
  5. R5 · Thematic tile (sport, cruise format, charter). Prefer an existing commercial page where the theme is the place's core (Galápagos and Alaska for cruises). A journal post is the canonical target only when it is the site's most complete answer and nothing commercial is warranted (Willamette, Horse Racing, Heli-Skiing). Otherwise, with distinct search demand, create an experience-detail page nested under the parent, one page per sport or format, never one per event.
  6. R6 · Destination + travel style ("Family Safari", "A Safari Honeymoon", "Kyoto in Season"). The destination page or region hub is canonical; the style lives in tile copy plus one targeted FAQ. Never create a /destinations/<place>-<style>/ page.
  7. R7 · Major attractions (Walt Disney World, Universal, Disneyland). One destination page per resort area, with stable section ids for each resort, beats a page per park: Disney World and Universal share /destinations/orlando/.
  8. R8 · Tile broader or narrower than the page. Link the page whose subject matches the tile's main noun; rename the tile if its label over-promises ("Italy & Europe" → Italy). A narrower tile (Santorini, Amalfi) links the country page without a fragment unless a stable section answers it on its own.
  9. R9 · Deep links. Only to a stable section that answers the tile by itself: #places, the new sitewide #itineraries, or a named section on an approved new page. An id is never invented for one tile alone, and every fragment is checked at build time.
  10. R10 · Competing pages. Before any CREATE, grep src/content-pages/ for the subject; if an existing page scores fit ≥2, enhance it instead. Two pages may not share a primary subject. A nested detail page goes one level more specific than its parent (venues, formats, calendars, costs) and never restates the parent's intro.

Recommendation 23 subjects link to an existing page with no content change beyond the sitewide #itineraries id and the generated "Featured in" strip. Tile-side copy fixes, such as a season or a superlative, are listed in each subject and in the matrix. Pages marked "Skipped review" were high-confidence reuse with the target untouched.

07Pages to enhance

Recommendation 18 existing pages get one consolidated enhancement each. Greece, Italy and Japan each collected several audit specs, now merged into one. Word budgets are against the 3,500 ceiling; hubs and journal posts have none. Where the two reviewers required changes, those changes override the spec above them.

S02 Peru (Inca Trail trek and Lima food) /destinations/peru/ P1 ADV-T2 · ADV-E2 · FWT-T7

One Peru canonical serves the trekking tile, the Inca Trail event-card and the Lima food tile. It gains one trek itinerary and a Lima-specific clause.

Consolidated enhancement

(1) Add a 5th itin-card 'Inca Trail to Machu Picchu' (~120 words) after 'Peru Classic': Lima, 2 Sacred Valley nights, the 4-day classic trail, Sun Gate, permit-lottery timing (cite machupicchu.gob.pe), and Salkantay/Lares as fallbacks. Put the reverse link to /experiences/adventure-active-travel/ inside its itin-desc. (2) Add ~20 words to FAQ 3 naming Salkantay/Lares. (3) Add ~25 words to the Lima card or Lima FAQ naming Central and Surquillo market, with a reverse link to /experiences/food-wine-travel/ wrapped on existing words. (4) Add id="itineraries". NEEDS MARK on any 'we plan' private-group claim.

Word headroom: 3,157 / 3,500 -> 343 headroom; spend ~165 -> ~3,322.

Conflict resolved: Lima food and Inca Trail trekking are two angles of one country page. A separate Lima page was rejected as thin. The event-card's 'two nights in Cusco' contradicts the page, which governs (Sacred Valley first).

Adversarial review
  • Duplication / SEO upheld with changes (a) Give the new 'Inca Trail to Machu Picchu' itin-card a real portrait: np-e-52-inca-stairway-cloud.jpg, or a ni- crop of e-52, with alt, width, height and decoding attributes. (b) In the same pass, correct all four Cusco-first acclimatization statements on /destinations/south-america/ (lines 11, 50, 127, 173), not just the itinerary. (c) Re-measure body words with the verifier's bodyWords() after the edit and keep the result at or below ~3,350 to leave margin.
  • Visitor intent upheld with changes (a) Trek itin-card sequence: Lima, then Sacred Valley 2 nights, then Cusco 1-2 nights, then the trail from KM82. This matches the page's protocol. Update the event-card correction to 'Sacred Valley first, then Cusco', not merely 'Sacred Valley first'. (b) Replace 'lottery' wording on the page and in the spec. Permits are released annually and sold only through licensed operators. Cite the Ministerio de Cultura / official Inca Trail permit source, and keep machupicchu.gob.pe for site tickets. (c) Budget a new ni- portrait (e.g. a crop of ev-adventure-active-travel-inca-trail-steps.jpg) for the itin-card. (d) Extend the South America hub correction to every 'Cusco first' instance (intro, Peru place-card, why-item, FAQ, itinerary). (e) Correct adventure exp-card 2 to 'one pass above 13,000 feet' or give accurate pass heights with a source. (f) Make the food tile's image swap to peru-lima.jpg mandatory.
S04 Active Iceland (Laugavegur, glacier walks) /destinations/iceland/ P2 ADV-T4

The Iceland country page is the canonical. It gets a trekking itinerary and supporting copy so the tile's activities are actually on the page.

Consolidated enhancement

(a) Add ~10 words to the Highlands card naming the Laugavegur. (b) New itin-card 'The Highlands on Foot' (~100 words): Laugavegur ~55 km over 4 days, optional Fimmvorduhals, a Vatnajokull glacier walk, Jokulsarlon kayak, June-September. (c) Why-item 'Glacier & Highland Guiding' (~70 words) carrying the reverse link to the adventure page. (d) FAQ 'Can we do the Laugavegur with lodge support?' (~60 words). Cite Vatnajokull National Park. Add id="itineraries". This needs one new ni- portrait. NEEDS MARK on operator claims.

Word headroom: 1,372 / 3,500 -> 2,128; spend ~250 -> ~1,620.

Adversarial review
  • Duplication / SEO upheld with changes (a) Use the existing np-e-54-vatnajokull-crevasse.jpg (or a ni- crop of e-54) for the new itin-card instead of commissioning a new portrait. (b) Reword the FAQ to hut-supported or guided hut-to-hut with bag transfer, and cite the hut operator or Vatnajokull National Park for distances and season. /destinations/orlando/ Orlando: Walt Disney World & Universal P1
Serves
FAM-T5 · FAM-T7
Primary intent
Decide how to build an Orlando theme-park week: Walt Disney World, Universal Orlando, or both. The questions it answers: how many park days, which on-site hotel and what it gets you (Disney early entry vs Universal Express Unlimited), Lightning Lane vs Express, which resort fits which ages and heights, and when to go. Nothing on the site serves this today. Grep of src/content-pages finds Orlando, Disney and Universal only in the family-travel tiles 5 and 7 and in one exp-tag on experiences.html.
Working title
Orlando | Walt Disney World & Universal — Hit Your Mark Travel (62 chars rendered; in the .astro prop write "&"). Fallback if it reads too long in SERPs: "Orlando: Disney World & Universal — Hit Your Mark Travel" (56).
Meta description
Disney World or Universal Orlando? Park days, on-site hotel perks, Lightning Lane vs Express Unlimited, and which rides fit kids under 48 inches. (145 chars)
H1
The layout renders the h1 from hero.headline. It should be a planning claim about the real decision, not a superlative. Direction: "Two Resorts.<br>One Week.<br>Decide the Split First." (or close to it). eyebrow: "Central Florida · USA". hero.sub (2–3 sentences): Disney's four theme parks and Universal's three theme parks plus Volcano Bay sit about 10–15 miles apart. Figures come from the official resort sites, and the mileage must be sourced or dropped. The planning work is the park-day split, the hotel that unlocks each resort's perks, and the booking windows. Name no rides in the sub. Use no "magical"/"ultimate" adjectives.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > North America > Orlando (built by DestinationLayout from name="Orlando" plus the region prop; BreadcrumbList JSON-LD matches the visible trail. Keep the name prop short, "Orlando", so the crumb is not "Orlando: Walt Disney World & Universal").
Template
DestinationLayout, region north-america ({label: "North America", href: "/destinations/north-america/"}). Two files: src/content-pages/destinations__orlando.html (body partial only) and src/pages/destinations/orlando/index.astro (wrapper copied from src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro). schemaType="website". Country/place page with a 4-crumb trail, so the 3,500 body-word ceiling applies. No pageCss. Hero stats are required: Best Season and Best For. Set them only from sourced facts. Candidates: Best Season "Jan – Feb · Late Apr – May · Sep – Nov" (the crowd/heat/hurricane reasoning must come from the season section's sources) and Best For "Families<br>& Multigen". hero.ctaSecondary should be {label: "Disney or Universal? ↓", href: "#disney-or-universal"}.
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (Home > Destinations > North America > Orlando) + TouristDestination (name Orlando, description = meta description, image = hero) + FAQPage (built from the pf-* block). Rules: no AggregateRating, no Event/Product/Offer for parks, no HowTo. Per SCHEMA-LIBRARY.md § 4 the layout emits all four, so no extra JSON-LD in the partial.
Answer capsule
40–70 words under the intro h2, answering "Disney, Universal, or both, and for how long?" Shape: Disney World has 4 theme parks and suits kids from about age 4. Universal has 3 theme parks since Epic Universe opened on May 22, 2025, and its headline rides need 40–48 inches, so it fits roughly 8 and up. A week split 4 Disney days, 2 Universal days and 1 pool day covers both. Sources for every number: park counts and the Epic Universe opening date come from disneyworld.disney.go.com and universalorlando.com (newsroom); the 40/48-inch heights from Universal's ride-height-requirements page (already linked on family-travel); "age 4" and "under-3s free" from Disney's ticket page. The 4/2/1 split is HYMT's recommendation and must be phrased as advice, not a statistic. Put a dated "Checked <Month> 2026" note nearby, since Typhoon Lagoon and Lightning Lane pricing are perishable.
Word target
2,400–3,000 body words (hard ceiling 3,500 for a 4-crumb destination page). Measure after build. The comparison section (~350) and 8 FAQs (~900) are the bulk. Do not pad.
Effort
large
Dependencies
1) Images: at least 4 new 16:9 place-card photos, plus np- intro, ni- itinerary, dc- and og- crops. This is a launch gate because a --photo grid fails without a real photo per card. 2) Fact check against official Disney/Universal pages in the build month (Typhoon Lagoon status, Lightning Lane windows, Express Unlimited scope, hotel counts), with visible 'Checked <Month> 2026' lines, and reconcile family-travel tiles 5 and 7 in the same PR. 3) NEEDS MARK answers (visit dates, credential) before any first-person or credential line. 4) Same-PR edits: experiences__family-travel.html tile hrefs (5 -> /destinations/orlando/, 7 -> /destinations/orlando/#disney-or-universal) plus the intro prose link; north-america hub prose/FAQ link; destinations.html card; multigenerational and north-america-destination-guide journal links; public/llms.txt 68->69. 5) Separate commit: tools/head-baseline.json update. 6) Coordinate with /destinations/disneyland/ (if built) on the llms.txt count and the reciprocal FAQ. 7) Branch from origin/main and merge origin/main before the PR. MANIFEST and sitemap conflicts resolve as a union. npm run build must pass (Node >=22.19).

FAQ candidates
  • Should we do Disney World or Universal Orlando with young kids? (Disney for under-8s; cite Universal height page for 40/48 in)
  • How many days do you need for Walt Disney World and Universal together? (park-day split, pool day; cross-link: the future /destinations/disneyland/ carries the reciprocal 'Disneyland or Disney World?' FAQ, one each, no more)
  • Is staying at a Disney resort hotel worth it? (30-min early entry at every park, Extended Evening Hours at deluxe/DVC, 60-day dining window for the whole stay; Disney sources)
  • Which Universal hotels include Express Unlimited, and does it work at Epic Universe? (Portofino Bay, Hard Rock, Royal Pacific; USF and IOA only; Universal source, dated)
  • How does Lightning Lane work at Disney World now? (Multi Pass vs Single Pass, purchase windows, prices vary by date; dated, link Disney)
  • What is the best time of year to visit Orlando's theme parks? (crowd/heat/hurricane windows; NOAA for hurricane season dates)
  • Is Typhoon Lagoon open? (closed for refurbishment since September 2026; Blizzard Beach status; dated 'checked' line + Disney link)
  • Do we need a car in Orlando? (Disney buses/Skyliner/monorail vs Universal walking paths/water taxis; source transport pages; transfers between resorts)
  • Is Orlando good for a multigenerational trip? (link /experiences/multigenerational-travel/; ECV/accessibility services cite Disney DAS page)

/destinations/st-lucia/ St. Lucia P1
Serves
AIV-T4 · AIV-E4 · BCH-T6
Primary intent
A single-island St. Lucia week. The page answers which side of the island to base on (Soufrière under the Pitons, or the north: Rodney Bay, Cap Estate, Castries), whether a boutique sanctuary beats an all-inclusive here, how a honeymoon is shaped, and what the transfer from Hewanorra (UVF) means for flight choice and resort choice. The Barbados + St. Lucia two-island trip stays on /destinations/barbados-eastern-caribbean/.
Working title
St. Lucia: Pitons, Soufrière or the North — Hit Your Mark Travel (64 chars incl. suffix; fallback "St. Lucia: Soufrière vs the North — Hit Your Mark Travel", 56 chars)
Meta description
Soufrière or the north, boutique or all-inclusive, and the long road from Hewanorra: planning a St. Lucia week under the Pitons, December to April. (147 chars)
H1
Use the hero headline as the single H1, the same way Turks & Caicos uses "The Best Beach in the Western Hemisphere Is Here." It should state the deciding fact, not use the brand or a label. Direction: the Pitons decide the view, and the airport decides the base. Example shape: "Two Pitons, Two Airports,<br>One Decision About Where to Sleep." Keep it under 70 characters with tags stripped. The hero sub (2–3 sentences) says plainly that the island is about 27 miles long (source: Government of Saint Lucia or CIA World Factbook), that long-haul flights land at Hewanorra in the far south, and that Soufrière and the north are two different holidays.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > Caribbean & Mexico > St. Lucia (built by DestinationLayout from the region prop; BreadcrumbList emitted from the same trail)
Template
DestinationLayout, region caribbean-mexico, 4-crumb country page. Files: src/content-pages/destinations__st-lucia.html (body only) + src/pages/destinations/st-lucia/index.astro, which copies src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro: schemaType="website", name="St. Lucia", region={{label:"Caribbean & Mexico", href:"/destinations/caribbean-mexico/"}}, a hero with eyebrow "Eastern Caribbean · Windward Islands", ctaPrimary "Plan a St. Lucia Trip" -> /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "Explore St. Lucia ↓" -> #places, and stats [{Best Season: "December – April"}, {Best For: "Couples<br>& Honeymoons"}]. No pageCss. Use the section classes from destinations__turks-caicos.html.
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items) + TouristDestination (name "St. Lucia", containedInPlace Caribbean & Mexico per SCHEMA-LIBRARY §4) + FAQPage generated from the page-faq block. All emitted by DestinationLayout. Do not add AggregateRating, Hotel/LodgingBusiness nodes for named resorts, or WebSite/SearchAction.
Answer capsule
40–70 words directly under the intro h2. Cover: the dry season runs December–April with May as shoulder, which matches the hero stat and the Barbados and Turks capsules. A week is the norm, 7 nights. The base decision is Soufrière (the Pitons, boutique, view-led) vs the north (Rodney Bay/Cap Estate, beaches, most all-inclusives). Long-haul flights land at Hewanorra (UVF) in the south. Source for each number: dry-season months from the Saint Lucia Meteorological Services climate normals or NOAA/NHC hurricane-season dates (June 1–Nov 30); the UVF-to-Soufrière and UVF-to-Rodney Bay road times only from the Saint Lucia Tourism Authority (stlucia.org) or SLASPA airport guidance, else NEEDS FIGURE (never estimate a transfer time); trip length from Mark's own booking pattern (NEEDS MARK if he hasn't stated one; Barbados's capsule uses '7–8 days' as a single-island week and can be mirrored only if Mark confirms). Do not put the UNESCO 2004 date in the capsule. It belongs on the place-card.
Word target
2,200–2,900 body words. The country page band is 1,500–3,500 and the hard build ceiling is 3,500, so stay well under it to leave room for later FAQ growth.
Effort
large. A new destination page with 10+ sections, 3–7 new photographs plus hub, dc, rc and og crops, edits to 5 existing pages (two experience pages, Barbados, the hub, the /destinations/ index, the journal guide), an llms.txt count change coordinated with S05, and a head-baseline commit.
Dependencies
1) Photography: at least 4 distinct real 16:9 place-card photos and 3 ni- portraits before the --photo grids can ship. No placeholder or duplicate cards. Also the hub 16:9, the dc- crop and ideally rc-/og- crops, all registered through tools/image-manifest.mjs. 2) Mark's input on the NEEDS MARK items, especially properties and cost ranges. 3) Sequencing with S05 for the llms.txt destination count. 4) Tile retargets on all-inclusive-vacations and beach-island-escapes, and the Barbados place-card retarget, land in the same PR as the page, so no href points to a missing route (the internal-links check fails the build otherwise). 5) Branch from origin/main and merge main before the build (CLAUDE.md branching rules). 6) A merge to main deploys production: verify with npm run verify:prod about 10 minutes after the merge.

Strip those so Barbados describes only itself. Anguilla is dropped everywhere (no page, no image); the beach-island tile is renamed 'St. Lucia'. No head-baseline changes are needed on existing pages unless a title or description changes. The all-inclusive description already names St. Lucia, so leave it. Do not create a separate St. Lucia honeymoon or all-inclusive URL. Both intents live on this page.

FAQ candidates
  • Is Soufrière or Rodney Bay the better base in St. Lucia?
  • How long is the transfer from Hewanorra (UVF) to Soufrière, and is a helicopter worth it?
  • Should I fly into Hewanorra or George F. L. Charles airport?
  • Is St. Lucia good for an all-inclusive, and how does it compare with Jamaica or the Dominican Republic?
  • Is St. Lucia a good honeymoon destination, and which side of the island suits a honeymoon?
  • When is the best time to visit St. Lucia, and how much does hurricane season matter?
  • Can you climb Gros Piton, and how hard is it?
  • How many nights do you need in St. Lucia, and is it worth pairing with Barbados? (answer briefly, then link to /destinations/barbados-eastern-caribbean/ for the pairing itinerary)
  • Do US travelers need a passport or visa for St. Lucia?
  • Which currency is used in St. Lucia, and are US dollars accepted? (Eastern Caribbean dollar, pegged at 2.70 to USD, per ECCB)
Authoritative sources to research
  • Saint Lucia Tourism Authority, https://www.stlucia.org/ (already cited on the Barbados page): areas, festivals, entry requirements, transfer guidance
  • UNESCO World Heritage Centre, Pitons Management Area (inscribed 2004), https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1161
  • Saint Lucia Air and Sea Ports Authority (SLASPA): Hewanorra (UVF) and George F. L. Charles (SLU) airport facts
  • Saint Lucia Meteorological Services and NOAA National Hurricane Center (season June 1–Nov 30, climatological peak)
  • US State Department country information page for Saint Lucia (passport/entry)
  • Eastern Caribbean Central Bank (EC$ peg 2.70 per USD)
  • Saint Lucia National Trust (Pigeon Island National Landmark) and Soufrière Foundation / Sulphur Springs Park for site facts
  • Gros Piton trail: Saint Lucia Forestry Department or the Gros Piton Tour Guides Association for the climb time and permit/guide requirement
  • CIA World Factbook, Saint Lucia (area, length)

/destinations/dolomites/ Dolomites P2
Serves
ADV-T5 · ADV-E3
Primary intent
A traveller who wants to walk the Dolomites hut-to-hut and is asking how: which Alta Via (1 or 2) or which base (Cortina, Alta Badia, Val Gardena), how many days, when the rifugi are open, how via ferrata fits in, how to get in and out, and whether a planner is worth it. The page is about trekking and season in the Dolomites, which is a different job from Italy's city, art and food intent and from the Europe hub's region overview.
Working title
Dolomites | Alta Via Hut-to-Hut Trekking — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Alta Via 1 and 2, rifugio nights, via ferrata and Val Gardena: how to plan a Dolomites hut-to-hut trek inside the short summer window when the huts are open. (157 chars)
H1
The layout builds the H1 from hero.headline, in the same short, declarative, line-broken style as Turks & Caicos ("The Best Beach in the<br>Western Hemisphere<br>Is Here."). Direction: the Dolomites are walked hut to hut, and the rifugio dinner is the reason to do it. For example, "Walk Hut to Hut.<br>Eat Like It Matters.<br>Sleep at 2,000 Metres." The altitude figure may only stay if a cited rifugio elevation backs it, otherwise cut it. Keep "Italy" out of the H1 and the title head. Eyebrow: "Italian Alps · Veneto & South Tyrol" (the provinces are to be verified against the route's actual footprint: Belluno and Bolzano, plus Trento for AV2). The hero sub (2–3 sentences) names the choice the page helps with: which Alta Via, or a base-and-day-hike week instead.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > Europe > Dolomites (built by DestinationLayout from the region prop; BreadcrumbList must match the visible trail)
Template
DestinationLayout, region Europe ({ label: "Europe", href: "/destinations/europe/" }), 4-crumb country/place page modelled on src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro + src/content-pages/destinations__turks-caicos.html. New files: src/pages/destinations/dolomites/index.astro and src/content-pages/destinations__dolomites.html. schemaType "website". Hero stats must carry "Best Season" (value set from the single cited rifugio window, e.g. "Mid-June – mid-September" only once it is sourced) and "Best For" (e.g. "Hut-to-Hut<br>Trekking"). ctaSecondary "Explore the Routes ↓" -> #places. Body ceiling 3,500 words (4-crumb page).
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (Home > Destinations > Europe > Dolomites) + TouristDestination (name "Dolomites", containedInPlace Italy; geo/sameAs to the UNESCO World Heritage listing "The Dolomites", inscribed 2009, whc.unesco.org/en/list/1237, only if the layout supports sameAs) + FAQPage built from the pf-item accordion, all emitted by DestinationLayout. Do not add AggregateRating or HowTo, and do not chase FAQ rich results.
Answer capsule
p.answer-capsule under the first h2 (intro-title), 40-70 words, stating the direct answer. The Dolomites are best walked hut to hut on the Alta Via 1 or 2, which run north-south through the range and sleep in staffed rifugi with half-board dinners. The huts open from [opening month] to [closing month] (one window, cited to CAI or the provincial tourism board: Dolomiti/Südtirol, Veneto/Provincia di Belluno, Trentino). A classic AV1 section takes about [N] days from Lago di Braies to Belluno, with Innsbruck or Venice as the gateway in and Venice the way out. Every number must come from a source. Hut season and AV1 length/stage count: CAI / provincial tourism board or the official Alta Via 1 route description (dolomiti.it / Provincia di Belluno). Do not reuse 'six days' or 'July-September' from the existing event-card without checking. Do not invent a price.
Word target
2,400-3,000 body words (hard ceiling 3,500 for a 4-crumb destination page; aim for about 2,800 so later FAQ additions have room)
Effort
large: new destination page plus wrapper, 10+ new images (hero, og, np, 4-5 place photos, 2-3 itin tiles, rc crop), primary-source research on the season, routes and hut rules, and Mark's input; plus coordinated edits to the adventure-active-travel exp-card, event-card and season copy, Europe hub and Italy links, llms.txt count, head-baseline commit, and MANIFEST appends.
Dependencies
Gated on (1) Mark's NEEDS MARK answers, especially whether the first-hand Dolomites experience is real; (2) photography/image generation, because a --photo place grid needs a real photo on every card and the CLAUDE.md rules forbid placeholders; (3) one season window sourced from CAI or a provincial tourism board before any copy is written. Coordinate with S10 on the llms.txt destination count and the hub count-free heading change; the second PR to merge rebases and sets the final number. Before building, branch from origin/main (fetch first), merge origin/main again before the PR, and run `npm run build` (verifier: title/description length, the >=2 journal links rule, no <a> inside .place-card/.exp-card, img attributes, under 3,500 words, llms.txt depth-1 counts). Until launch, the adventure exp-card keeps /plan-your-trip/ as its interim target.
Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) /destinations/italy/ (3,235 words; title 'Rome, Tuscany & the Amalfi Coast'; cards Rome/Tuscany/Amalfi/Venice/Sicily/Puglia) serves art, food and city intent. The new page keeps 'Italy' out of the title head, H1 and description, and targets Alta Via, rifugio and via ferrata terms, which Italy never uses. Italy gets a short contextual link (under 40 words, to stay below 3,500). 2) /destinations/europe/ mentions the Dolomites in one clause only. The new page becomes the canonical answer, and the hub links to it rather than growing its own Dolomites section. 3) /experiences/adventure-active-travel/ exp-card 5 currently bundles the Dolomites with 'Alps' and the Tour du Mont Blanc. Renaming it to 'Dolomites' and pointing it here avoids a scope mismatch. TMB stays an unlinked mention on the experience page; no Alps/TMB page is created, because a broad Alps page would collide with Italy and France. 4) Season disagreement: event-card 'July – September', experience season-strip 'mid-June through late September', spec 'mid-June to mid-September'. The brief requires one window cited to CAI or a provincial board, applied in the same PR to the hero Best Season stat, the seasons-strip, the FAQ, the adventure event-card 'when' and the adventure season-strip copy. 5) Routing error: the event-card's 'Flying into Venice and out of Innsbruck' is reversed for standard north-to-south AV1. Correct it on the event-card and do not carry it into the itin-card. 6) Gateways are not a place-card ('Venice/Innsbruck gateways' dropped as thin); they live in the intro and the airport FAQ. 7) llms.txt count collision with S10 (see hubAndNavChanges).
Hub, nav, llms.txt
public/llms.txt line 16 '68 destination guides': do not hard-code 68->69. Increment the destination count by one at merge time, reconciled with S10. Whichever of S05/S10 ships second sets the final number (70 if both ship), and the hubs' count-free heading change happens once, not twice. /destinations/europe/: add an in-text link (FAQ line ~299) and optionally a place-card and related-card if a real dc-/rc- photo exists. Do not restructure the hub's six-country grid without design sign-off. Footer: src/components/Footer.astro lists region hubs only (Europe), so no footer change. Main nav: no change. Adventure experience page: rename exp-card 5, relink it, and fix the event-card season/routing and the season-strip window (experience page title/description unchanged, so no baseline change for it). New page: add its title/description/canonical to tools/head-baseline.json via `node tools/verify-deployment.mjs --update-baseline` in its own commit. dist/sitemap-0.xml gains one <url> (reflowed by format-sitemap.mjs).
Sections, in order
  1. Hero (layout props): eyebrow, headline, sub, ctaPrimary 'Plan a Dolomites Trek' -> /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary -> #places, stats Best Season + Best For. Hero image passed as preloadImage. See imageGaps: a real hero is needed.
  2. section.intro-section > .intro-content > h2.intro-title (first h2) + p.answer-capsule (40-70 words) + .intro-body paragraphs (the AV1 vs AV2 difference in plain terms, what a rifugio night actually includes, why the season is short). .intro-image.intro-image--photo with an np- portrait (gap).
  3. section.places-section#places, h2 'Where to Walk' style. .places-grid.places-grid--even.places-grid--photo with 5 place-cards (each an <a class=place-card>, no nested <a>), every card with a real photo: (a) Alta Via 1 corridor (Lago di Braies to Belluno), (b) Alta Via 2 corridor (Bressanone/Brixen to Feltre; more via ferrata and exposure), (c) Tre Cime di Lavaredo / Sexten Dolomites, (d) Cortina d'Ampezzo (base town, 2026 Winter Olympics host, summer lift access), (e) Val Gardena & Seceda / Alta Badia (base-and-day-hike option for travellers who do not want to change huts nightly). No 'Venice/Innsbruck gateways' card: gateways go in the capsule-adjacent intro and in an FAQ. Place-card hrefs cannot be self-anchors to nothing; follow the Turks & Caicos pattern for what place-cards link to (plan-your-trip or an in-page itinerary anchor), and keep any new anchor attached to a named section.
  4. section.itineraries-section, 2-3 .itin-card with .itin-image--photo: (i) 'Dolomites Hut-to-Hut' (the event-card trip: AV1 in about 6 rifugio nights, run north to south, Braies to Belluno; arrive via Innsbruck or Venice, depart Venice; days to be sourced, not copied from the event-card, whose 'into Venice, out of Innsbruck' routing is reversed for standard AV1); (ii) 'Base-Camp Week: Val Gardena & Alta Badia' (one or two hotels, lift-assisted day hikes, optional guided via ferrata); (iii) optional 'Dolomites + Venice' finish linking to /destinations/italy/. .itin-duration/.itin-meta: days only, no prices unless sourced.
  5. section.why-section, h2 on what planning changes, .why-grid of 4 .why-item: Rifugio booking lead time (popular huts fill months ahead: cite the rifugio or CAI booking guidance); Luggage transfer vs carrying a pack (which operators/huts allow it: NEEDS SOURCE); Via ferrata: guide, kit (helmet, harness, lanyard), grading; Choosing the AV1 vs AV2 vs base-camp format by fitness.
  6. Optional div.cost-range block (as on Turks & Caicos): rifugio half-board per night and guide day-rate ranges ONLY if sourced from CAI/rifugio published tariffs or a named operator. Otherwise omit the block, never estimate.
  7. section.seasons-strip, h2 'Dolomites by Season', .seasons-grid with 3-4 .season-item: Early season (mid/late June: snow on north-facing passes, some huts not yet open); Peak (July-August: all huts open, busiest, afternoon thunderstorms); Late season (September until the huts close: the best-value window); Winter (ski, the huts the page covers are closed). The one cited opening window sets the badge 'Best Overall Window' and the hero Best Season stat.
  8. section.page-faq with .pf-grid of 6-10 .pf-item (button.pf-q in h3.pf-q-h, aria-expanded/controls; .pf-a role=region). First sentence answers in 25 words or fewer.
  9. Read-more block (.read-more, .read-more__grid, 2-3 .read-more__card) linking journal posts. This satisfies the >=2 journal links rule: /travel-journal/the-case-for-shoulder-season/, /travel-journal/private-guide-advantage/, /travel-journal/glacier-express-field-report/ (the Alps by rail, for the 'add Switzerland' extension), /travel-journal/european-grand-tour-mistake/ (slow travel versus the city circuit).
  10. section.dest-cta (.dest-cta__title, __body, btn-primary-gold -> /plan-your-trip/, __note).
  11. section.related-section .related-grid, 3 .related-card with rc- crops: /destinations/italy/, /destinations/europe/ (or /destinations/france/ for the Chamonix/TMB angle), /experiences/adventure-active-travel/. Cards are anchors with no nested <a>. Decorative img alt="" + aria-hidden.
FAQ candidates
  • When are the Dolomites rifugi open? (the one cited window; early/late-season snow caveat)
  • Alta Via 1 or Alta Via 2: which should I walk? (AV1 gentler and more popular; AV2 longer, more exposed, more via ferrata; cite the route authority)
  • How many days do I need for the Alta Via 1? (full route vs the classic northern section; sourced stage count)
  • Which airport do I fly into for the Dolomites? (Innsbruck, Venice Marco Polo/Treviso, Verona, Bolzano; AV1 runs north to south, so in via Innsbruck/Braies and out via Venice/Belluno)
  • Do I need a guide or experience for via ferrata? (grading, kit, when a certified guide is advisable; cite Collegio Guide Alpine / CAI)
  • Can my luggage be transferred between huts? (where it is possible, where it is not; sourced)
  • How far ahead should rifugi be booked? (cite hut booking guidance; NEEDS MARK for his own lead-time practice)
  • What is a night in a rifugio actually like? (dorm vs private rooms, half-board, showers, sheet sleeping bag requirement; cite CAI hut rules)
  • Can I do the Dolomites without hiking hut to hut? (base-camp option in Val Gardena / Alta Badia / Cortina with lift-assisted day hikes)
  • Is the Dolomites trek suitable for a family or a first multi-day hike? (fitness, daily elevation gain from sourced stage data)
Authoritative sources to research
  • Club Alpino Italiano (CAI), cai.it: rifugio rules, hut network, via ferrata safety and grading
  • Dolomiti UNESCO Foundation (dolomitiunesco.info) and UNESCO World Heritage Centre listing 1237 'The Dolomites' (inscribed 2009)
  • Provincial tourism boards for the hut season window and route data: Südtirol/South Tyrol (suedtirol.info), Veneto/Dolomiti Bellunesi (infodolomiti.it / dolomiti.it), Trentino (visittrentino.info)
  • Official Alta Via 1 / Alta Via 2 route descriptions (stage lists, start/end points Lago di Braies -> Belluno; Bressanone -> Feltre)
  • Individual rifugio websites for opening dates and half-board tariffs (e.g. Rifugio Lagazuoi, Rifugio Nuvolau): cite named huts only
  • Collegio Guide Alpine (Alto Adige / Veneto) for via ferrata guide practice
  • Parco Naturale Tre Cime / Sexten Dolomites (Drei Zinnen) official site
  • Airport/transfer facts: Innsbruck Airport, Venice Marco Polo; Dolomiti Bus / SAD regional bus timetables for trailhead access

/destinations/oaxaca-mexico-city/ Oaxaca & Mexico City Owner-gated P2
Serves
CUL-T6
Primary intent
A culture-led trip to interior Mexico (Oaxaca City and the Valles Centrales plus Mexico City), not a beach week. The reader wants to know how to split the days between the two cities, what to see (Monte Albán and Mitla, mezcal palenques, Teotitlán del Valle weaving, Centro Histórico murals, the Museo Nacional de Antropología, Coyoacán and the Casa Azul), when to go (dry season, Día de Muertos, Guelaguetza) and what a guided version costs. Search targets: "Oaxaca Mexico City itinerary", "Oaxaca luxury travel", "Mexico City cultural trip travel advisor", "Day of the Dead Oaxaca trip". This intent appears nowhere else on the site. The audit grep for oaxaca|monte alb|mexico city|mezcal|frida|uxmal finds only the culture tile and one passing line in european-grand-tour-mistake.
Working title
Oaxaca & Mexico City — Hit Your Mark Travel (43 characters including the suffix; written "Oaxaca & Mexico City — Hit Your Mark Travel" in the wrapper, the way Turks & Caicos is written. A longer fallback if the owner wants the culture term in the title: "Oaxaca & Mexico City: Cultural Mexico — Hit Your Mark Travel", 60 characters.)
Meta description
Monte Albán, the mezcal palenques of the Valles Centrales and Mexico City's Anthropology Museum: splitting 8–10 days, and booking Día de Muertos (Nov 1–2). (155 chars)
H1
The H1 is the hero headline, which DestinationLayout renders from hero.headline. Make it a 2–3 line statement of the page's answer that names both cities and sets the culture-first frame against the coast, for example along the lines of "Mexico's Culture Lives Inland" or "Two Cities, Three Thousand Years". Any year span must be sourced (Monte Albán founding c. 500 BCE per UNESCO WHC 415). Do not write a superlative such as "the most culturally concentrated" unless it is attributed. hero.sub (2–3 sentences) says what the planning work is: how to split the nights between Oaxaca and Mexico City, which valley villages to visit on which days, and how to time the trip around Día de Muertos or the Guelaguetza. Exactly one h1, and the body starts at h2.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > Caribbean & Mexico > Oaxaca & Mexico City (the layout builds it from the region prop. The BreadcrumbList JSON-LD matches the visible trail automatically. The trail cannot vary by referrer, so the way back to /experiences/culture-immersive-travel/ goes in body copy and the FeaturedIn strip.)
Template
DestinationLayout, 4-crumb country/place page. New files: src/content-pages/destinations__oaxaca-mexico-city.html (body sections only) and src/pages/destinations/oaxaca-mexico-city/index.astro, modeled on src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro. Props: schemaType="website", name={"Oaxaca & Mexico City"}, region={{ label: "Caribbean & Mexico", href: "/destinations/caribbean-mexico/" }} (same parent as riviera-maya-los-cabos, so the hub and breadcrumb agree), hero.eyebrow "Interior Mexico", hero.image /assets/img/e-65-monte-alban-gran-plaza.jpg (also passed as the preload/LCP image, so never loading=lazy), ctaPrimary "Plan an Oaxaca & Mexico City Trip" -> /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "See the Places ↓" -> #places, hero.stats exactly two: { label: "Best Season", value: "October – April" (owner-confirmed; the dry season must be cited to SMN/CONAGUA climate normals) } and { label: "Best For", value: "Culture<br>& Food" }. No pageCss. Ceiling 3,500 body words.
Schema
The layout emits WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items) + TouristDestination + FAQPage. FAQPage is extracted from the page-faq block, so there is no hand-written JSON-LD. TouristDestination name "Oaxaca & Mexico City". If the layout supports containsPlace or touristType, list Oaxaca de Juárez, Monte Albán, Mexico City, with touristType cultural/heritage. Do not add AggregateRating, HowTo or SearchAction. og:image must be the new og-e-65-monte-alban-gran-plaza.jpg (1200x630, with og:image:alt), which does not exist yet. Until it does, the default image applies.
Answer capsule
Write it under the intro-section h2, in 40–70 words. It should answer "how do I plan a culture trip to Oaxaca and Mexico City" in one self-contained block: the best window, how to split the trip, and the timing traps. - Best window: October to April. Source: SMN/CONAGUA climate normals for Oaxaca and CDMX. - Trip length and split: for example "8–10 days, 4–5 nights in Oaxaca". This is an HYMT planning claim, so it needs the owner's confirmation before it ships. - Flight time: the CDMX–Oaxaca hop of about one hour. Source: the Aeroméxico or Viva published schedule, with the date checked. Never estimate it. - Timing traps: Día de Muertos runs October 31 to November 2 (source: UNESCO ICH listing 00054 or the Mexican government's cultura.gob.mx), and the Museo Nacional de Antropología closes on Mondays (source: mna.inah.gob.mx). Use at least one of these numbers. No invented figures, and no superlative unless it is attributed.
Word target
2,200–2,800 body words. The country-page band is 1,500–3,500, and the 4-crumb ceiling fails the build above 3,500. Do not pad: 6 place-cards at about 70–90 words each, 3–4 itineraries at about 80 words, 4 why-items, a 4-item seasons strip, a cost block, and 8 FAQs at 60–200 words each.
Effort
large
Dependencies
1) Owner gate: the page is conditional on Mark confirming that HYMT plans interior Mexico (P2, owner-gated). Without that, it falls back to retitling the tile to the Yucatán and linking riviera-maya. 2) Image generation and crops (about 9–10 new files plus og-, dc- and rc- crops), registered through tools/image-manifest.mjs. The --photo grids cannot ship without them. 3) Sourced figures: INAH hours and fees, festival dates for the current year, SMN seasons, the US State Dept advisory levels (dated), and the cost band with its verified date. 4) Ideally after Phase B (tile map, FeaturedIn, apply-tile-links) and Phase C, so the tile href flips through the map. If it ships earlier, edit the tile href by hand in the same PR. 5) One PR containing the page, the llms.txt 68→69 change, the hub and index cards, NAME_EXCEPTIONS, riviera-maya and journal inbound links, the cost-ranges entry, and images. head-baseline goes in its own commit. 6) Branch from origin/main, merge main before running analyze or build, and run npm run build plus verify:prod about 10 minutes after merge.
Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) /destinations/riviera-maya-los-cabos/ is the only Mexico page. It owns the Yucatán & Mérida card, the 'Coast and Culture' itinerary and the Mayan-site and cenote intent. The new page includes no Yucatán place-card or itinerary, routes all Yucatán, Chichén Itzá, Uxmal and Mérida interest to riviera-maya by link, and keeps its own scope to Oaxaca plus central Mexico. Optional Teotihuacan coverage stays within CDMX. 2) URL naming: /destinations/mexico/ is avoided on purpose so the 'Mexico' head term is not split across riviera-maya, the caribbean-mexico hub and a new page. Naming the URL for the two cities keeps the intent distinct. The title also avoids a bare 'Mexico Travel' lead. 3) /destinations/caribbean-mexico/ is a regional hub. Adding a 7th card means its 'Six Destinations…' heading must change to 'Seven'; do not repeat the Asia hub's mismatch of 7 cards under a 'Six' heading. The hub title and description list six islands, so any edit to them is its own head-baseline commit. 4) Tile-label mismatch: the culture tile says 'North America', but /destinations/north-america/ has no Mexico content. The label is aligned to the Caribbean & Mexico parent instead of adding Mexico to the North America hub. 5) /experiences/food-wine-travel/ and /experiences/culture-immersive-travel/: the brief links to them rather than repeating their pitch. The cuisine and mezcal angle stays place-specific here. 6) Cost block: the riviera-maya cost entry already cites the IVA via SAT. Reuse the same authority so the two Mexico pages stay consistent, but give this page its own range and drivers. 7) No existing journal post covers this subject, so nothing is duplicated. A future 'Día de Muertos in Oaxaca' journal post would be a natural companion, but it is not part of this brief.
Hub, nav, llms.txt
These all land in the same PR as the page. 1) public/llms.txt: '68 destination guides' becomes '69 destination guides' (the build fails otherwise). Also add the page to the destination list under Caribbean & Mexico if llms.txt lists children. 2) src/content-pages/destinations__caribbean-mexico.html: add a 7th place-card with a dc- crop, and change the h2 'Six' to 'Seven'. 3) src/content-pages/destinations.html: add a dest-card with data-region="caribbean-mexico". 4) src/pages/destinations/index.astro: add the NAME_EXCEPTIONS entry "oaxaca-mexico-city": "Oaxaca & Mexico City". 5) Footer: no change. The Destinations column is hubs only. 6) tools/head-baseline.json: one new entry for the page, in its own commit via --update-baseline. Any hub title or description edit gets its own head-baseline commit. 7) dist/sitemap-0.xml gains a <url>. 8) tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs: a new entry. 9) Once the tile map exists, a src/data/tile-map.json record for culture-immersive-travel/6 with target /destinations/oaxaca-mexico-city/. The FeaturedIn strip then renders the back-link to Culture & Immersive Travel automatically, using the <!-- featured-in --> marker above the related-section. 10) The /experiences/ hub card tags for the Culture card may be refreshed separately (audit hubCardMismatch). This page does not require it.
Sections, in order

  2. places-section id="places" > h2 'Six Places Worth Planning Properly' (heading count must equal card count) > places-grid places-grid--even places-grid--photo, 6 a.place-card (no nested <a>; real photo on every card or drop --photo): (a) Oaxaca City Centro [Oaxaca] - Santo Domingo, markets, food; (b) Monte Albán & Mitla [Valles Centrales] - Zapotec sites, INAH hours; (c) Mezcal & Craft Villages [Valles Centrales] - Santiago Matatlán palenques, Teotitlán del Valle weaving, San Bartolo Coyotepec barro negro; (d) Mexico City Centro Histórico [CDMX] - Templo Mayor, Palacio Nacional Rivera murals, Bellas Artes; (e) Chapultepec, Roma & Condesa [CDMX] - Museo Nacional de Antropología, food neighborhoods; (f) Coyoacán, San Ángel & Xochimilco [CDMX south] - Casa Azul (timed tickets), Anahuacalli. FAQ candidates
  • How many days do I need for Oaxaca and Mexico City? (first sentence a complete answer; split owner-confirmed)
  • Should I start in Mexico City or Oaxaca? (altitude ~2,240 m in CDMX vs ~1,550 m Oaxaca per INEGI; flight connection)
  • When is the best time to visit Oaxaca? (dry season Oct-Apr per SMN; festival trade-offs)
  • How far ahead should I book Oaxaca for Día de Muertos? (Oct 31-Nov 2; lead time is NEEDS MARK)
  • Is Oaxaca safe for travelers? (cite US State Dept Mexico travel advisory by state, dated; Oaxaca and CDMX levels as published that day)
  • How do I get from Mexico City to Oaxaca - fly or drive? (flight ~1 hr vs ~6-7 hr by road via ADO/highway 150D; cite carriers/SCT)
  • Do I need tickets in advance for Frida Kahlo's Casa Azul? (yes, timed online tickets per museofridakahlo.org.mx)
  • Is the Anthropology Museum worth a full day, and what day is it closed? (closed Mondays per INAH/MNA)
  • Can you visit Monte Albán and Mitla in one day? (distances/hours per INAH)
  • Is a mezcal palenque visit suitable if I don't drink? (what a palenque visit involves; NEEDS MARK on the producers used)
Authoritative sources to research
  • UNESCO World Heritage Centre: Historic Centre of Oaxaca and Archaeological Site of Monte Albán (WHC 415); Historic Centre of Mexico City and Xochimilco (WHC 412); Pre-Hispanic City of Teotihuacan (WHC 414); Central University City Campus of UNAM (WHC 1250, if murals at CU are mentioned); Prehistoric Caves of Yagul and Mitla (WHC 1352)
  • UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage: Indigenous festivity dedicated to the dead (00054); Traditional Mexican cuisine (00400)
  • INAH (inah.gob.mx) for site hours, closures and admission fees at Monte Albán, Mitla, Templo Mayor and Teotihuacan, and the Museo Nacional de Antropología (mna.inah.gob.mx) for its Monday closure and hours
  • Museo Frida Kahlo / Casa Azul (museofridakahlo.org.mx) for timed tickets and the Anahuacalli combined ticket
  • Consejo Regulador del Mezcal (CRM) and the NOM-070-SCFI-2016 mezcal standard for the categories ancestral, artesanal and mezcal, and the Denominación de Origen states
  • Oaxaca state government / Secretaría de Turismo de Oaxaca for the Guelaguetza dates (the Mondays after July 16), checked for the current year
  • SMN/CONAGUA climatological normals for Oaxaca and Mexico City (the dry and rainy season months)
  • INEGI for the elevations of Mexico City (~2,240 m) and Oaxaca de Juárez
  • US Department of State, Mexico Travel Advisory (state-by-state levels), dated on the page
  • SAT, Ley del IVA Article 1 (16% IVA), the same source the riviera-maya-los-cabos cost block already uses; Oaxaca and CDMX lodging-tax (ISH) rates from the state finance ministries
  • Aeroméxico and Viva published schedules for the MEX–OAX flight time

Serves
SAF-T6
Primary intent
Planning a luxury Namibia trip: which regions go together (Sossusvlei/Namib, Damaraland, Skeleton Coast, Etosha), which month, whether to fly between camps or self-drive the distances, and what it costs. The secondary intent is "Namibia vs Botswana / Namibia as the add-on" for a second Africa trip. The page sells the landscape safari: desert-adapted wildlife and dunes. It does not sell volume game-viewing, which Botswana and Kenya & Tanzania already cover.
Working title
Namibia | Sossusvlei & Etosha — Hit Your Mark Travel (52 chars, counted with the & decoded; write & in the .astro). Rejected: "Namibia | Sossusvlei, Etosha & Skeleton Coast — Hit Your Mark Travel" (68, over the limit) and "Namibia — Hit Your Mark Travel" (30, legal but sits on the floor and wastes the distinguishing terms). The pattern matches "Rwanda | Mountain Gorilla Trekking — Hit Your Mark Travel".
Meta description
Etosha's waterholes peak July–October. Sossusvlei's dunes, desert-adapted elephant in Damaraland, and when flying between camps beats self-driving Namibia. (155 chars)
H1
The layout renders the H1 from hero.headline, so the H1 is a position statement, not a label. It should combine the landscape-first claim with the distance problem, along the lines of "the safari where the drive between the parks is part of the trip, and the reason to fly some of it". Use <br> breaks as in the Turks & Caicos headline. Do not repeat Zambia's "second Africa trip" framing. Do not use "breathtaking", "unforgettable" or any other word from the CONTENT-STANDARDS § 2 banned list. hero.sub (about 60 words) names Sossusvlei, Etosha and the Skeleton Coast and states the planning decision: fly-in, self-drive, or a mix.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > Africa > Namibia. DestinationLayout builds it from the region prop, so the visible trail and the BreadcrumbList cannot disagree.
Template
DestinationLayout, region={{ label: "Africa", href: "/destinations/africa/" }}, 4-crumb country page (3,500-word ceiling). Files: src/content-pages/destinations__namibia.html (body only) and src/pages/destinations/namibia/index.astro, which mirrors src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro with schemaType="website", name="Namibia", hero.image, eyebrow "Southern Africa", ctaPrimary "Plan a Namibia Trip" → /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "Explore the Regions ↓" → #places, and stats [{Best Season}, {Best For}]. No pageCss. Cost block comes from a new "destinations__namibia.html" row in tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs, same shape as the Botswana row: range, includes, excludes, drivers.
Schema
The layout emits WebPage, BreadcrumbList (4 items), TouristDestination (name "Namibia") and FAQPage (parsed from the page-faq markup by extractFaq) automatically, per SCHEMA-LIBRARY.md § 4 (destination row). No extra JSON-LD. No AggregateRating, no HowTo, no SearchAction. Set dateModified to the cost-block verification date.
Answer capsule
The capsule answers "What is a Namibia trip and how do you plan it?" in 40–70 words, directly under the intro h2. Name the four anchor regions (Sossusvlei, Damaraland, Skeleton Coast, Etosha), the best window, and the fly-in vs self-drive trade-off. Use at least one number, each from a named source, never invented: - Etosha's size (about 22,270 km²) from MEFT / Namibia Wildlife Resorts. - Sossusvlei's dune heights, or the Namib Sand Sea's World Heritage inscription and area, from UNESCO WHC (site 1430). - Namibia's population density, among the lowest on earth, from the Namibia Statistics Agency or the World Bank. - The Windhoek–Sossusvlei–Etosha road distance from the Roads Authority or a mapped route. - The dry-season months from the Meteorological Service of Namibia. Link the figure used to its source inline. No first-hand claim in the capsule unless Mark confirms one.

2,400–3,000 body words. The country ceiling is 3,500; keep at least 400 words of headroom for the FeaturedIn strip and later FAQ growth. Sibling pages land at 2,900–3,400, so do not pad to match them.

Conflicts and how the page avoids them
- Botswana (/destinations/botswana/). The Kalahari overlap is real: Botswana owns the Central Kalahari card, the desert-lion itinerary and the 'Kalahari Desert' framing. The Namibia page gets no Kalahari place-card. When it mentions the Kalahari it links to Botswana. It competes on the Namib Desert, dunes and the coast, which no existing page covers. Cross-link both ways (related-card swap). - Zambia & Victoria Falls. That page claims 'the Second Africa Trip, Not the First'. Namibia must not reuse that positioning. Differentiate as the landscape-and-desert-wildlife trip; Zambia stays the walking-and-river trip. Victoria Falls appears only as a combination leg linking out, never as a place-card. - Safari experience page. Exp-card 6 copy ('Desert-adapted elephant and rhino… Etosha's waterholes in the dry season') must be served by the Damaraland and Etosha cards plus the season strip. Do not import that page's inconsistent cost figures ($1,200–$3,200 vs $700–$1,500 per night). Namibia gets its own dated row. - Africa hub. Its 'Six African Destinations' heading and the safari page's 'Six Countries We Know Inside Out' both hard-code counts. The hub needs a retitle when a 7th card is added. The safari h2 stays true because Path A keeps six tiles. - Jordan/Wadi Rum, Middle East dunes, and Atacama (South America journal). Desert-landscape siblings, but different continents and intents, so no cannibalisation. Use the existing journal comparison as an inbound link only. - docs/seo/photography-plan.md line 142 says Namibia has 'no library asset'. e-38 exists and is wired, so that line is stale. Note it; do not edit it in this change. - Path A vs Path B. If the owner picks Path B, nothing in this brief ships. The tile goes to /destinations/zambia-victoria-falls/, and the safari meta description, hub tags and llms.txt stay as they are.

FAQ candidates
  • When is the best time to visit Namibia? (Dry season for Etosha vs green season; months sourced from the Meteorological Service of Namibia.)
  • Should we self-drive Namibia or fly between camps? (Distances, gravel roads, tyre and insurance realities; a charter leg's cost against days saved. Advisory figures from the Roads Authority / US State Dept road-safety page.)
  • How many days do you need in Namibia? (Circuit length by region pairing; the answer depends on fly-in vs road.)
  • Is Etosha as good as a Botswana or Kenya safari? (Honest comparison: waterhole concentration and a self-drive park vs private-concession density; link /destinations/botswana/ and /destinations/kenya-tanzania/.)
  • Can you combine Namibia with Botswana and Victoria Falls? (Routing via the Zambezi Region/Kasane; link /destinations/zambia-victoria-falls/.)
  • Is Namibia malaria-free? (Coast and Sossusvlei vs northern Etosha/Zambezi Region; cite CDC Yellow Book / Namibia Ministry of Health.)
  • Do US citizens need a visa for Namibia? (Namibia moved to visa requirements for US citizens in 2025. Verify current e-visa/visa-on-arrival rules with Namibia's Ministry of Home Affairs and travel.state.gov, show a 'verified as of' date, and add a verify-before-travel line.)
  • Is Namibia good for families with children? (Malaria-light southern circuit, minimum ages at lodges and on activities NEEDS MARK, long transfers.)
  • What does a luxury Namibia trip cost? (Range from the cost-range block, dated; NEEDS FIGURE.)
  • Where can you see desert-adapted elephant and black rhino? (Damaraland/Kunene, with the conservancy model credited to Save the Rhino Trust Namibia / NACSO.)
Authoritative sources to research
  • Ministry of Environment, Forestry and Tourism (MEFT), Namibia: park entry fees and rules for Etosha, Namib-Naukluft and Skeleton Coast
  • Namibia Wildlife Resorts (NWR): Etosha and Sossusvlei gate times and park facts
  • UNESCO World Heritage Centre: Namib Sand Sea (1430) and Twyfelfontein (1255)
  • Namibia Meteorological Service: seasonal rainfall and temperature for the season strip and Best Season stat
  • Namibia Ministry of Home Affairs, Immigration, Safety and Security, plus US State Department travel.state.gov country page: visa/entry rules and road-safety advisories
  • CDC Yellow Book / CDC malaria map for Namibia
  • Namibia Roads Authority: distances and road classes (gravel C/D roads)
  • Namibia Tourism Board (visitnamibia.com.na): regional overview
  • NACSO (Namibian Association of CBNRM Support Organisations) and Save the Rhino Trust Namibia: communal conservancies and desert-adapted rhino
  • International Dark-Sky Association / DarkSky International: NamibRand Nature Reserve designation
  • Namibia Statistics Agency or World Bank: population density

/destinations/disneyland/ Disneyland & the Orange County Coast Owner-gated P3
Serves
FAM-T6
Primary intent
A West Coast family planning a Disneyland long weekend. They want to know how many days both parks need, how date-based tickets and park reservations lock the calendar, whether to stay at one of the three Disney hotels or off-site, why Disneyland is the stronger Disney resort for children under six, and which Orange County beach town to add. Search shape: "Disneyland trip planning", "Disneyland with toddlers", "how many days at Disneyland", "Disneyland long weekend", "Disneyland vs Disney World" (shared with Orlando, so handled by one comparison FAQ).
Working title
Disneyland & the Orange County Coast — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Disney advises at least three days for Disneyland's two Anaheim parks. Park reservations, the three Disney hotels, and why it is the stronger pick for under-sixes. (163 chars)
H1
The H1 is hero.headline (DestinationLayout renders it as h1.dest-hero__headline). Use a 2-3 line claim in the house voice, built on the page's thesis and not a superlative. Direction: the original Disney park is the compact one, sized for a long weekend and for small children. Example shape only: "The Original Disney Park<br>Is the One Built<br>for Small Children." It must not repeat the title verbatim. hero.sub (about 60-80 words) carries the specifics: two parks on one esplanade, Disney's own "at least three days" advice, and date-based tickets plus park reservation. Primary CTA "Plan a Disneyland Trip" -> /plan-your-trip/. Secondary "Explore the Parks ↓" -> #places.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > North America > Disneyland & Orange County. The layout builds it from region + name. BreadcrumbList follows the same trail array automatically. It cannot vary by referrer, so a family-travel visitor returns via the body link and FAQ link to /experiences/family-travel/ (and the FeaturedIn strip once phase B ships).
Template
DestinationLayout country page, 4-crumb, region={ label: "North America", href: "/destinations/north-america/" }. Files: src/pages/destinations/disneyland/index.astro (wrapper modelled on src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro) + src/content-pages/destinations__disneyland.html (body partial modelled on src/content-pages/destinations__turks-caicos.html). name prop "Disneyland & Orange County" (breadcrumb leaf and TouristDestination name via plain(name)). hero.eyebrow "Southern California". hero.stats limited to the two required, both sourced: Best Season (crowd/price windows from Disneyland's own dated ticket calendar, else NEEDS FIGURE) and Best For (e.g. "Under-Sixes<br>& Long Weekends", which rests on the height-requirement count). No flight-time stat. No pageCss.
Schema
DestinationLayout's set, unchanged: WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items, matching the visible trail) + TouristDestination (name "Disneyland & Orange County", nominative use of the Disney name only; containedInPlace per the layout's region handling) + FAQPage built from the page-faq block. Do not add: AggregateRating, Event or Product nodes for parks or tickets, an Organization node for Disney, logos, or HowTo. Canonical, og and twitter tags are inherited from Base.astro. og:image comes from a new og-anaheim-disneyland-pier-golden-hour.jpg entry in src/lib/og-crops.ts, generated by tools/make-og-crops.mjs and never hand-edited. Until that exists, it falls back to /assets/og-default.jpg.
Answer capsule
Place p.answer-capsule directly under the first h2 (intro-title), at 40-70 words. Answer directly how long a Disneyland trip needs and who it suits best. Required numbers, each from a named source and never estimated: (1) 'at least three days' for both parks, from Disneyland's own trip-length page, https://disneyland.disney.go.com/destinations/visit/, which family-travel already cites; (2) the count of attractions with no height requirement ('more than 60' as the family-travel tile states), recounted from Disneyland's official height-requirements listing on the build date and dated on the page; (3) optionally, 'two parks' and 'three Disney hotels' from Disneyland's parks and hotels pages. Shape: two parks on one esplanade in Anaheim, three days per Disney, a long weekend for West Coast families, and the stronger Disney resort for under-sixes because of the no-minimum attraction count. Close with the order of operations: lock dated tickets and the park reservation before the flights. Do not compare drive times or crowd levels with numbers unless they are sourced.
Word target
2,400-2,800 body words. The country-page ceiling is 3,500 and the build fails above it, so leave headroom of at least 500 words for the FeaturedIn strip and future FAQ edits. Budget: intro + capsule about 350, places about 650 (6 x ~105), itineraries about 400, why about 220, seasons about 200, CTA about 70, cost-range about 180, FAQ about 700 (8 x ~85), read-more about 60.
Effort
large

The page is P3 and conditional. 2) /destinations/orlando/ must ship first. FAQ candidates
  • How many days do you need at Disneyland? (Disney advises at least three for both parks; source: Disneyland visit page.)
  • Is Disneyland or Walt Disney World better for young children? (Comparison FAQ: answer for under-sixes, then link /destinations/orlando/ for the full Orlando case; the Orlando page carries the mirror FAQ linking back.)
  • Do I need a park reservation as well as a ticket? (Source: Disneyland's ticket and park-reservation pages; date the answer.)
  • Is it worth staying at a Disney hotel at Disneyland? (Walking distance, any hotel-guest entry benefit, cost against Anaheim off-site; benefits from Disney's hotel pages only.)
  • What is the best time of year to visit Disneyland? (Ticket-tier calendar low-demand windows; Halloween and holiday overlay dates.)
  • Can you do Disneyland as a long weekend? (Yes for West Coast families; a 3-day shape plus travel days.)
  • How many Disneyland rides have no height requirement? (Count from the official height-requirements list, with the date.)
  • Is Park Hopper worth it at Disneyland? (Answer from the parks' layout plus Disney's Park Hopper rules; no invented time savings.)
  • Which beach town should we add after Disneyland? (Newport/Balboa vs Laguna/Dana Point vs San Diego; distances only from a cited source such as Google Maps or Caltrans, otherwise left out.)
  • When should we book a Disneyland trip? (Ticket and reservation availability, hotel booking windows per Disney; HYMT lead-time advice flagged NEEDS MARK if experience-based.)
Authoritative sources to research
  • Disneyland Resort trip-length/visit page: https://disneyland.disney.go.com/destinations/visit/ (already cited on experiences__family-travel.html)
  • Disneyland official height requirements / attractions list (count of no-minimum attractions; recount on build date)
  • Disneyland theme park tickets, ticket-tier calendar and Theme Park Reservations pages (date-based pricing, reservation rule, low-demand windows)
  • Disneyland Resort hotels pages: Disneyland Hotel, Disney's Grand Californian Hotel & Spa, Pixar Place Hotel (count and hotel-guest benefits)
  • Disneyland Lightning Lane / Disney Premier Access and dining reservation pages (booking windows, dated)
  • Disneyland seasonal events pages (Halloween Time, Holidays) for overlay dates
  • Visit Anaheim (visitanaheim.org) for district and off-site context
  • Visit Newport Beach, Visit Laguna Beach, Visit Dana Point official tourism boards for the coast cards
  • San Diego Tourism Authority (sandiego.org) for the La Jolla/San Diego card
  • NOAA / National Weather Service Los Angeles office for the May-June marine layer ('June gloom') and seasonal temperatures
  • John Wayne Airport (SNA) and LAX official sites for airport options (no invented flight or drive times)
  • Walt Disney World early-entry page (disneyworld.disney.go.com/guest-services/early-entry/) only for the Disneyland-vs-Disney-World FAQ contrast
