/destinations/american-southwest/ American Southwest Owner-gated P3
Serves
WEL-T5
Primary intent
A US traveler choosing a high-end Southwest trip, who wants to know which base (Tucson destination spa, Sedona, or southern Utah canyon country), when to go, and how to combine them. Destination-spa programs (Canyon Ranch Tucson, Miraval Arizona) are the wellness angle, red rock and the national parks are the landscape angle. This page is the canonical target for wellness-retreat-travel exp-card 5 ("Arizona & Southwest"), which currently points at /plan-your-trip/. Intent is distinct from /experiences/wellness-retreat-travel/ (a global comparison of six wellness regions), from /travel-journal/north-america-destination-guide/ (a continent overview with one Southwest card) and from /destinations/aspen/ (Colorado Rockies ski). CONDITIONAL: build only if the owner confirms the practice books the Southwest often enough to stand behind a full guide and an "itineraries we plan" section. If he declines, the tile resolves to /travel-journal/north-america-destination-guide/ (the Southwest card has no stable id; ask for id="american-southwest" on that island-card) or stays an explicit exception in tile-map.json.
Working title
American Southwest | Sedona, Utah & Spas — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Sedona red rock, Utah's five national parks and destination-spa weeks at Canyon Ranch and Miraval in Tucson, planned for the October-to-April window. (149 chars)
H1
The H1 comes from the hero headline in the wrapper (DestinationLayout renders it), using the 2-3 line <br> pattern of Turks & Caicos. Direction: a specific claim that the Southwest is three different trips (a spa week in Tucson, red rock in Sedona, Utah's canyon parks) and the planning work is choosing and sequencing them. No superlative without a source. Exactly one h1. The body opens at h2.
Breadcrumb
Home > Destinations > North America > American Southwest (built by DestinationLayout from the region prop; the BreadcrumbList JSON-LD renders from the same trail)
Template
DestinationLayout, region north-america, 4-crumb country/place page. Files: src/content-pages/destinations__american-southwest.html (body sections only) and src/pages/destinations/american-southwest/index.astro, modelled on src/pages/destinations/turks-caicos/index.astro: schemaType="website", name="American Southwest", region={{label:"North America", href:"/destinations/north-america/"}}, hero {image, eyebrow "Arizona · Utah", headline, sub, ctaPrimary "Plan a Southwest Trip" -> /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "Explore the Region ↓" -> #places, stats [{Best Season: "October – April"}, {Best For: "Spa Weeks<br>& Canyon Country"}]}. Use only stats with a source: Best Season comes from NPS park climate pages plus the existing north-america-destination-guide fact, and leave out any flight-time stat. No pageCss. Word ceiling is 3,500 body words.
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items, matching the visible trail) + TouristDestination (name "American Southwest", containedInPlace North America hub; touristType wellness and outdoor travelers; includesAttraction only for places named in the place-cards) + FAQPage (from the page-faq block, 6-10 Q&As). All are emitted by DestinationLayout per SCHEMA-LIBRARY.md § 4. No AggregateRating, no HowTo, no SearchAction. Brand names (Canyon Ranch, Miraval, Amangiri) appear as text only, never as Hotel/LodgingBusiness nodes the site cannot vouch for.
Answer capsule
40-70 words under the first h2, answering "When and how should I plan a luxury Southwest trip?" Direct answer: go October to April, pick a base by purpose (Tucson spa week, Sedona red rock, Utah canyon parks), and do not try all three in under 10 days. Allowed numbers and their required sources: "five national parks in Utah" (NPS / Utah Office of Tourism 'Mighty 5'); "7-10 day loop connects them" (already stated in /travel-journal/north-america-destination-guide/, but re-verify against NPS drive distances); Canyon Ranch or Miraval minimum stay or package length (canyonranch.com/offers, miravalresorts.com); summer average high in Phoenix or Tucson or on a canyon floor (NWS climate normals or NPS Zion weather page). Use at least one; never round or estimate.
Word target
2,400-3,000 body words (country-page ceiling 3,500; leave at least 150 words of headroom for the FeaturedIn strip and future edits). Roughly: intro 300, places 6x90=550, itineraries 4x110=450, why 250, seasons 250, cost-range 200, FAQ 8x70=560, plus headings, CTA and read-more.
Effort
Large. It is a full new destination page plus 10-14 new images (none exist beyond e-49), a cost-range data row, hub and llms.txt count changes, and the owner-gate interview. Estimate 1 PR, content ~1.5 days after the owner's answers, plus image sourcing.
Dependencies
1) Owner approval (P3 gate) and answers to the NEEDS MARK items. 2) Phase B architecture merged first: tile-map.json, tools/apply-tile-links.mjs, the tile-links verifier check, and FeaturedIn in DestinationLayout. Phase C (reuse) should ship before this Phase D page. 3) Licensed photography for the hero, place-cards, itinerary tiles and the rc-/dc-/og- crops, appended through tools/image-manifest.mjs. 4) A cost-ranges-data.mjs row with dated sources. 5) llms.txt 68->69 in the same PR. 6) A head-baseline update in its own commit. 7) An edit to /travel-journal/north-america-destination-guide/ adding the inbound link, plus an id="american-southwest" only if it serves as the fallback target. 8) Branch from origin/main, merge main before build, and Node >=22.19.0.
Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) /travel-journal/north-america-destination-guide/ already holds a ~200-word Southwest card (Mighty 5, Amangiri, gateways LAS/PHX, Oct-Apr, key properties). Keep the journal card as the overview teaser and link it to the new page. The new page goes deeper (bases, sequencing, spa programs, permits, costs, FAQ) and does not reuse the journal's sentences verbatim. Facts must agree: Oct-Apr window, Amangiri 12+ months, LAS/PHX gateways. If the new page's verified facts differ, fix the journal in the same PR. 2) /experiences/wellness-retreat-travel/ compares six wellness regions and should not be re-argued. This page links up to it for the global comparison and keeps the spa section Arizona-specific. Its cost-range already cites Canyon Ranch packages; reuse the same source and date-check the figures so they agree. 3) /destinations/north-america/ FAQ 6 already answers Southwest timing in one line. Keep the two consistent and link it here. 4) /destinations/aspen/ covers Colorado; Dunton Hot Springs (CO), mentioned in the journal, stays out of this page's scope, or appears only as a cross-link to Aspen. 5) There is no single-state page (Arizona/Utah/Sedona) and none should be created, since that would cannibalise this page; Sedona and Zion stay as place-cards and do not get their own URLs. 6) Owner-gating: if the owner declines, do not build the page. The tile then needs a fallback target, the journal card, which requires a new stable id ("american-southwest") on that island-card. Record either outcome in tile-map.json.

Cite NWS and NPS for temperatures and the North Rim seasonal closure.

FAQ candidates
  • When is the best time to visit the American Southwest? (NPS park weather pages; NWS normals)
  • Canyon Ranch or Miraval: which destination spa suits which traveler? (official program pages; NEEDS MARK for the judgment)
  • How many nights does a destination-spa week need, and what is included in the package? (Canyon Ranch / Miraval package terms)
  • Do I need reservations or timed-entry permits for Zion, Arches or the Grand Canyon? (nps.gov current reservation rules, dated)
  • Can I combine Sedona, Tucson and Utah's national parks in one trip, and how long does it take? (drive times, Google/ADOT/UDOT; 7-10 days for Utah parks alone)
  • Which airport should I fly into: Phoenix, Tucson, Las Vegas or Salt Lake City? (gateway fact already on the NA journal guide; airline-neutral)
  • How far ahead should I book Amangiri or Enchantment Resort for spring and fall? (journal says Amangiri 12+ months; NEEDS MARK to confirm)
  • Is the Southwest too hot in summer, and what about the monsoon? (NWS Phoenix/Tucson monsoon season Jun 15-Sep 30)
  • Is the Southwest good for a first family national-parks trip? (only if Mark confirms; else drop)
  • Do I need a guide for Antelope Canyon or the slot canyons? (Navajo Nation Parks & Recreation: Antelope Canyon by tour only)

09New experience-detail pages

Recommendation Six nested pages, each one level more specific than its parent:

All six depend on one template change, the optional ExperienceLayout parent prop (§11). That change ships first, in its own PR. Golf and Horse Racing are treated differently on purpose: horse racing has no page because the Kentucky Derby report, extended with an "Other meetings" section on Royal Ascot and the Dubai World Cup, already answers it; golf has distinct playing-trip demand that the Masters report does not serve.

/experiences/sports-event-travel/tennis/ Tennis (Wimbledon, Roland Garros, Australian Open) P1
Serves
SPT-T1 · SPT-E1
Primary intent
Commercial-investigation intent for Grand Slam access: how to actually get a seat at Wimbledon, and what it costs. The page compares debenture vs official hospitality vs the public ballot and the Queue, with lead times. It then gives the Roland Garros and Australian Open equivalents, plus dates and where to stay. The page earns its place through the side-by-side comparison across all three routes and all three tournaments. The AELTC, FFT and Tennis Australia sites each describe only their own products and never compare across tournaments. It is not a narrative explainer.
Working title
Wimbledon & Grand Slam Tennis Travel — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Wimbledon debenture, official hospitality or the ballot? The three routes compared, plus Roland Garros (late May) and the Australian Open (January). (148 chars)
H1
The H1 is the hero headline, which the layout renders. Make it a short promise built around the seat decision, not "luxury tennis". For example: "Centre Court, Philippe-Chatrier, Rod Laver Arena", or "Three Slams, Three Ways In". Keep it to one h1, written in the existing <br> two-line style. The eyebrow is "Sports & Event Travel · Tennis". The hero sub must name the three routes: debenture, official hospitality, ballot.
Breadcrumb
Home > Experiences > Sports & Event Travel > Tennis. Both the visible trail and the BreadcrumbList are rendered from the same trail array once the parent prop lands, and the parent crumb links to /experiences/sports-event-travel/. A page under /experiences/<parent>/<child>/ that emits only 3 crumbs must fail the build. That needs a verifier fixture in tools/verify-checks.test.mjs.
Template
ExperienceLayout, nested at src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/tennis/index.astro with content in src/content-pages/experiences__sports-event-travel__tennis.html. It needs the new optional parent prop described in rules-data-template-qa.md section 11. Use section 11's prop shape, parent?: { label: string; href: string }, not the {name, path} the verifier text uses, so the plan has one spec. Pass parent={{ label: "Sports & Event Travel", href: "/experiences/sports-event-travel/" }}, name="Tennis", schemaType="website". Hero image: /assets/img/e-23-wimbledon-pimms.jpg, which is currently unused in src. Pass it as preloadImage through the hero, the same way the layout does today. Hero ctaSecondary should be "Compare the routes ↓", pointing at #wimbledon. Use only the existing experience.css classes. No pageCss, no new CSS.
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items: Home, Experiences, Sports & Event Travel, Tennis) + Service (name and serviceType "Tennis", provider = Organization @id, description = meta description) + FAQPage, extracted automatically from the page-faq block by extractFaq. Nothing else. No AggregateRating, and no SportsEvent/Event nodes: HYMT does not sell the tickets, and per-year event markup would go stale. Do not chase FAQ rich results. The FAQPage node is emitted only because the layout emits it.
Answer capsule
40-70 words directly under the first h2. Answer: 'How do you actually get good seats at Wimbledon, Roland Garros and the Australian Open?' Name the three routes in one sentence: a debenture seat, official hospitality, or the public ballot or sale. State the one fact that separates them, for example that a Wimbledon debenture is the only transferable Centre Court ticket. Include at least one number, all taken from AELTC pages: the number of Centre Court debentures in an issue, the 5-year debenture term, or when the ballot opens and closes. Do not use the parent's '6-18 months' figure unless an authority supports it for tennis. If the number cannot be verified from AELTC, FFT or Tennis Australia at writing time, leave a NEEDS FIGURE comment rather than a guessed number. No adjectives in place of facts.
Word target
1,800-2,400 body words, of which the three comparison tables plus their notes are roughly 40%. There is no ceiling for experience pages, but stay well under 3,500 for parity with country pages. Do not pad with narrative.
Effort
large
Dependencies
1) Prerequisite, in its own PR. Add parent?: {label, href} to ExperienceLayout and insert it into the trail array between Experiences and the name. Run gitnexus impact on ExperienceLayout and the breadcrumbs() function first, and report the blast radius: all 12 experience pages. Prove the output for the existing 12 pages is byte-identical by building and diffing dist/. Add a verifier check plus a fixture in tools/verify-checks.test.mjs that fails when a nested /experiences/<parent>/<child>/ page lacks the 4-crumb trail. 2) Generate an og- crop for e-23-wimbledon-pimms.jpg and add it to OG_CROPS. Generate any new np-/ev-/court images via the image pipeline, with MANIFEST.json entries written through tools/image-manifest.mjs. 3) Source research: AELTC, FFT and Tennis Australia pages for every figure in the tables, with dated 'checked on' lines. 4) Mark's answers to the NEEDS MARK items before any package or seat claim goes live. 5) Inbound-link edits to the parent, uk-ireland, france and australia (watch Australia's 3,389/3,500 words). Make these after the page exists, in the same PR as the page or immediately after, plus a head-baseline commit if any description changes. 6) Merge origin/main and run npm run build before opening the PR.
Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) The parent /experiences/sports-event-travel/ is the only competitor. It keeps the tile, a one-line capsule clause, and a shortened Wimbledon event-card that links down. The tennis page owns the 'Wimbledon debenture / hospitality / ballot' queries, so the parent must not carry a comparison. 2) S52 rejected a football page as a thin explainer against official hospitality sites. This page avoids the same fate only through the cross-route, cross-tournament comparison tables with costs and lead times, which AELTC, FFT and Tennis Australia do not publish. If the tables cannot be sourced, the page should not ship. 3) No per-tournament URLs (/wimbledon/, /roland-garros/), because those would be doorway pages. Each tournament is a section with a stable id: #wimbledon, #roland-garros, #australian-open. 4) The destination pages (uk-ireland, france, australia) keep the city-stay intent. The tennis page links to their #places sections and does not describe the cities. 5) The template mismatch both verifier lenses flagged: there is no parent prop today. The page must not ship until the layout change lands, or it would emit a 3-crumb BreadcrumbList that skips its parent. 6) Prop naming: the verifier said {name, path} and section 11 says {label, href}. Follow section 11.
Hub, nav, llms.txt
llms.txt: no count change. The page is nested at depth 2, so '12 trip types' stays true. Optionally add one line under the sports entry listing the nested page, if llms.txt lists sub-pages. Experiences hub /experiences/: no new card, and its ItemList stays at 12. Footer: no change. The Experiences column lists the depth-1 trip types only. Sitemap: picked up automatically, then reflowed by tools/format-sitemap.mjs. Head baseline: add the new page's title, description and canonical with node tools/verify-deployment.mjs --update-baseline in its own commit. If the parent's description is trimmed, that also goes into a baseline update. Plan record: add to src/data/tile-map.json (when it exists) sports-event-travel/exp-card/1 targeting /experiences/sports-event-travel/tennis/, and the event-card record with anchor #wimbledon.
Sections, in order
  1. intro-section. label 'Why Tennis Travel'. First h2, for example 'Three Grand Slams, Three Different Ways In', then the p.answer-capsule, then 2-3 intro-body paragraphs. The required 'who this is for / who it is not for' paragraph goes here as an intro-body paragraph, because no experience page has a dedicated class for it. Not for: anyone who wants a guaranteed seat on a specific court on a specific day for under the debenture-resale price, or anyone unwilling to commit to dates 6-12 months out. Use the intro-image--photo portrait slot. This is a gap: no np- tennis portrait exists.
  2. exp-cards-section, id="wimbledon". This is THE comparison section, and its id is what the parent's #wimbledon deep link targets. h2 like 'Wimbledon: Debenture vs Hospitality vs Ballot'. The comparison goes in a plain <table> inside the section (rows: route, what you get, which courts, how sold, typical lead time, transferable?, source). Columns: Centre Court debenture; No.1 Court debenture; Official Hospitality (AELTC-appointed caterer packages); the AELTC Public Ballot; the Queue; Ticketmaster/official resale. Every figure is cited to AELTC (wimbledon.com tickets, debentures pages) or the Wimbledon Debenture Holders' official ticket exchange. No exp-card grid with <a> children: if cards are used, they are whole-card anchors with no nested links, and each needs a real photo under exp-cards--photo. Prefer the table plus 3 short intro-body notes.
  3. Second content block, id="roland-garros". Reuse exp-cards-section, or an events-section block with one event-card. h2 like 'Roland Garros: Loges, Hospitality and the Public Sale'. Comparison table of FFT box/loge and official hospitality vs the public ticket release vs official resale, with dates, citing FFT/rolandgarros.com. One sentence plus a link to /destinations/france/#places (Paris card) for where to stay. Visible link text: 'planning Paris around the fortnight'.
  4. Third block, id="australian-open". h2 like 'Australian Open: Rod Laver Arena Packages and Ground Passes'. Table of Tennis Australia official premium/hospitality vs reserved seating vs ground pass, citing ausopen.com / Tennis Australia. Link /destinations/australia/#places (Melbourne & the Great Ocean Road card), anchor text like 'Melbourne before or after the Open'.
  5. events-section 'Trips We Plan Often'. 3 event-cards using the existing event-when/event-name/event-desc/event-meta/event-cta markup. (a) Wimbledon fortnight + London: CTA /plan-your-trip/, body links to /destinations/uk-ireland/#places. (b) Roland Garros + Paris/Champagne. (c) Australian Open + Melbourne/Great Ocean Road. Each package description carries a NEEDS MARK, and each needs a real ev- 3:4 photo. Only ev-e-27-wimbledon-pimms-terrace exists, and the parent already uses it. Generate ev- crops for Paris and Melbourne, or drop to an events grid with a photo on every card.
  6. inclusions-section 'What We Handle Around the Seat'. 4 inclusion-items: hotel within reach of SW19 / Porte d'Auteuil / Melbourne Park, transfers vs the District line / Metro / tram (cite TfL and PTV for travel times), dining, and rest days between sessions. The copy must not repeat the parent's generic four inclusions word for word.
  7. seasons-strip 'The Grand Slam Calendar'. season-items: January Melbourne (Australian Open, 2 weeks), late May to early June Paris (Roland Garros), late June to July London (Wimbledon), and optionally late August to September New York (US Open), as a mention only with no section. Mark the next edition's season-item--best only if dates are confirmed. Each date comes from the governing body's published calendar, with the 'checked on' date shown on the page.
  8. exp-cta. Same pattern as the parent: 'Plan My Trip' to /plan-your-trip/, ghost button back to /experiences/sports-event-travel/ ('All sports and events').
  9. cost-range, inserted the same way as tools/p3-8-cost-insert.mjs. Basis: priced by the seat per day, as on the parent. Figures only from AELTC (published debenture prices), FFT or Tennis Australia published package prices, with a dated source line. If no published price is found, use cost-range__figure--unit wording and a NEEDS FIGURE comment, never an estimate.
  10. page-faq. 6-10 items in the exact pf-item / h3.pf-q-h / button.pf-q / div.pf-a role=region markup, with ids pf-tennis-1..n. The first sentence of each answer must be a complete answer of 25 words or fewer.
  11. read-more. At least 2 journal links, which the verifier requires. masters-field-report: 'how access to a sold-out event actually works', the closest analogue to debentures. what-a-travel-advisor-actually-does: what an advisor can and cannot get you. The third should be europe-destination-guide, or the-case-for-shoulder-season, framed as staying on after the fortnight. Avoid the kentucky-derby report unless it is the only fit.
FAQ candidates
  • What is a Wimbledon debenture, and can a visitor buy a single day's seat? (AELTC; Debenture Holders' official exchange)
  • How does the Wimbledon public ballot work, and when does it open? (AELTC ballot page, with dates shown on the page)
  • Is the Wimbledon Queue realistic for show-court seats? (AELTC Guide to Queueing)
  • What is the difference between Wimbledon Official Hospitality and buying a debenture seat? (AELTC hospitality page)
  • Are resale or secondary-market Wimbledon tickets valid? (AELTC conditions of sale, which say tickets are non-transferable except debentures)
  • When are Roland Garros and the Australian Open, and which week is best to attend? (FFT / Tennis Australia calendars; first week = more matches across courts, second week = marquee matches; cite the order of play structure)
  • How do Roland Garros hospitality and public tickets go on sale? (FFT)
  • Where should I stay for Wimbledon, Roland Garros or the Australian Open? (Answer with distance or transit facts from TfL / RATP / PTV; NEEDS MARK for hotel preferences)
  • Is there a dress code at Wimbledon? (AELTC: none for spectators except in some hospitality and debenture areas; verify)
  • Can I combine a Grand Slam with a wider trip? (Answer briefly, then link uk-ireland, france, australia)
Authoritative sources to research
  • AELTC / wimbledon.com: tickets overview, Public Ballot, Guide to Queueing, Debentures (current issue, term, number of seats), Official Hospitality, Conditions of Sale, Championships dates
  • The All England Lawn Tennis Ground plc debenture issue documents (prospectus) for the term and price of the current series
  • Wimbledon Debenture Holders' official ticket exchange: how debenture seats are legitimately resold
  • FFT / rolandgarros.com: ticketing calendar, Official Hospitality and Loges, official resale, tournament dates
  • Tennis Australia / ausopen.com: ticket on-sale dates, Rod Laver Arena / Marvel Arena seating, premium and hospitality packages, Ground Pass, dates
  • Transport for London (Southfields / Wimbledon stations), RATP (Porte d'Auteuil), Public Transport Victoria (Melbourne Park trams): travel-time facts
  • ATP/WTA calendars only to confirm the Slam order. The governing bodies above remain the primary source for dates

/experiences/sports-event-travel/formula-1/ Formula 1 P1
Serves
SPT-T3 · SPT-E3
Primary intent
Commercial-investigation intent for a Formula 1 weekend: 'Monaco Grand Prix packages', 'F1 Paddock Club', 'Monaco GP yacht / balcony hospitality', 'Monza / Singapore Grand Prix hospitality'. The visitor already knows which race they want. They need four answers: which way to watch (Paddock Club, grandstand, yacht, balcony/terrace), when the race falls, how far ahead to commit, and what sets the price. The parent page covers sports travel across six sports. This page covers the three circuits named on the tile (Monaco, Monza, Singapore), plus Abu Dhabi as a one-card pointer to the UAE page.
Working title
Formula 1 Grand Prix Hospitality — Hit Your Mark Travel (55 chars incl. suffix). Alternative: 'Formula 1 Hospitality & Grand Prix Travel — Hit Your Mark Travel' (64 chars). It needs & in the Astro prop as the parent uses, so check that the verifier counts the decoded string.
Meta description
Formula 1 weekends at Monaco, Monza and Singapore: Paddock Club, grandstand, yacht and balcony tiers, the 6–18 month lead time, and what sets the price. (152 chars)
H1
The layout hero headline is the h1. Write a short, specific line about the race weekend rather than a superlative, e.g. a two-line 'Trackside at Monaco, / Monza and Singapore' shape. Do not repeat the parent's 'The Best Seat in the House'. Eyebrow: 'Sports & Event Travel · Formula 1'. Sub: one sentence naming the four ways to watch (Paddock Club, grandstand, yacht, balcony) and the lead time. ctaSecondary: 'The Circuits ↓' to #monaco.

Home › Experiences › Sports & Event Travel › Formula 1 (paths /, /experiences/, /experiences/sports-event-travel/, leaf). The layout renders it from the same trail array as the BreadcrumbList, so it needs the S47 parent prop.

No AggregateRating, no Event schema (we do not sell the tickets as an organiser, and dates change), no HowTo.
Answer capsule
40–70 words under the first h2 that directly answer 'how do I plan a Formula 1 weekend at the level worth flying for'. It should name the four ways to watch, say that the best hospitality commits 6–18 months out, and say that the seat rather than the hotel is the largest line. Allowed numbers and their sources: '6–18 months' is HYMT's own stated figure, already on the parent capsule and FAQ 1, so keep it consistent. A three-day weekend structure (practice / qualifying / race) comes from formula1.com. The number of rounds in the current season must come from the formula1.com published calendar, dated. Do not state prices, Paddock Club fees or yacht charter rates unless they come from a cited official page or Mark's real quotes.
Word target
1,800–2,400 body words. The experience pages have no word ceiling, but keep it substantive rather than padded. The #monaco block is the longest at about 300–400 words, Monza and Singapore about 150–250 each, and Abu Dhabi about 60–80.
Effort
large
Dependencies

Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) Cannibalisation with the parent on 'Monaco Grand Prix packages'. Fix: move the two Monaco-specific ACM drivers and the ACM source out of tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs 'experiences__sports-event-travel.html' (and the rendered cost-range block, lines ~227-240) onto this page's new row. Replace them on the parent with sport-agnostic drivers (e.g. which event and tier, party size and ages, single day versus multi-day) backed by its own cited source, e.g. Wimbledon debenture/hospitality or Royal Ascot enclosure terms, so the parent's cost basis stays valid. Cut FAQ 4's Monaco example to one linked clause. Leave the parent's capsule and its figures (6–18 months) untouched. The parent keeps one F1 tile and one Monaco event card that point here. 2) /destinations/uae-gulf/ owns the Abu Dhabi GP as a rate driver. This page gives Abu Dhabi one short card and a link and does not repeat the rate copy. 3) There is no Monaco or Singapore destination page, and none should be created (thin). Singapore gets no destination link because /destinations/vietnam-southeast-asia/ has no Singapore coverage. 4) Date accuracy: the parent's Monaco event card says 'May · Monte Carlo, Monaco'. The 2026 Formula 1 calendar moved Monaco to early June. Verify against formula1.com and fix the parent's event-when in the same PR if it is stale. The F1 cancellation FAQ answers the race-specific case and links to it rather than restating CFAR advice.

/experiences/cruises/yacht-charters/ Private Yacht Charters P1
Serves
CRU-T6 · CRU-E4
Primary intent
Commercial-investigational. The visitor has settled on a private, whole-boat crewed charter rather than a cabin on someone else's ship, and wants to know how it works before they enquire: which boat type (Turkish gulet, sailing or power catamaran, motor yacht), what the crew does, how the contract and the APA (Advance Provisioning Allowance) work, how the week is priced, and which cruising ground suits which month. The page serves cruises exp-card 6 "Private Yacht Charters" (Turkish gulets, Greek catamarans, Caribbean motor yachts, crew and chef) and, through #gulets, cruises event-card 4 "A Gulet of Your Own" (7-day crewed gulet on the Turquoise Coast). It does not cover shared small-ship or cabin cruising, which belongs to the parent and to /experiences/cruises/mediterranean/.
Working title
Private Crewed Yacht Charters — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Crewed gulets, catamarans and motor yachts, chartered by the week in the Med or the Caribbean: how crew, contract and the APA work, and what moves the price. (157 chars)
H1
The H1 is the hero headline that ExperienceLayout renders, and it is the only H1. Name the format and the ownership, e.g. "Your Boat, Your Crew,<br>Your Anchorages". Do not reuse the parent's "The Right Ship Changes Everything". The hero sub, in one or two sentences, names the three vessel types (gulet, catamaran, motor yacht), says the whole boat and crew are yours for the week, and names the two grounds (the Mediterranean and the Caribbean). No superlatives.
Breadcrumb
Home › Experiences › Cruises & Yachts › Private Yacht Charters. The layout builds it from the new parent prop. The visible trail and the BreadcrumbList render from the same trail array, so they match by construction. Every crumb is a link except the last, which is the current page.
Template
ExperienceLayout, nested under /experiences/cruises/. Files: src/pages/experiences/cruises/yacht-charters/index.astro (wrapper modelled on src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/index.astro) and src/content-pages/experiences__cruises__yacht-charters.html. Props: title, description, name="Private Yacht Charters", the new optional parent={ label: "Cruises & Yachts", href: "/experiences/cruises/" } (the label must match the parent wrapper's name prop exactly), schemaType left at the default, hero.image "/assets/img/e-07-yacht-cove.jpg" (also passed as preloadImage), eyebrow "Cruises & Yachts", ctaPrimary "Plan My Charter" → /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "A Gulet of Your Own ↓" → #gulets. Use only the existing experience.css and section-shared.css classes. No pageCss.
Schema
WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items: Home, Experiences, Cruises & Yachts → /experiences/cruises/, Private Yacht Charters) + Service (serviceType "Private yacht charter planning"; provider is the Organization @id; areaServed is the Mediterranean and the Caribbean, with no invented coordinates) + FAQPage, which extractFaq builds from the page-faq block. All four are emitted by ExperienceLayout and required by schema-required on the /experiences/ prefix. No AggregateRating, Offer prices, HowTo or TouristTrip.
Answer capsule
Put 40–70 words under the first h2 that answer the question 'how does a private crewed charter work?' The capsule should say four things. (1) You hire the whole vessel and its crew, usually for a week. The Saturday-to-Saturday norm comes from MYBA/charter-broker association guidance. (2) The charter fee is the base, and on top of it you pay an APA deposit of a stated percentage, drawn down for fuel, food, drinks and berthing, with the balance reconciled and returned. The percentage must come from MYBA, CYBA or IYBA published guidance and must not be invented; if no published figure is found, say 'a percentage set in the contract' and drop the number. (3) Gulets, catamarans and motor yachts suit different grounds. (4) The seasons: the Caribbean runs outside NOAA's 1 June–30 November Atlantic hurricane season (nhc.noaa.gov), and the Mediterranean window comes from the parent page's May–October framing, cross-checked against the gulet NEEDS MARK. The NOAA dates are the guaranteed number. No first-person experience claims go in the capsule.
Word target
1,800–2,400 body words. Experience pages have no ceiling, but one level more specific than the parent means depth on contract, APA, boats and seasons, not padding. Reverse-link edits on destination pages must add 0 words: Greece is at 3,115 and Barbados & Eastern Caribbean at 3,316 against the 3,500 ceiling.
Effort
medium. Page copy plus a sourced noBand cost row, 8 FAQs, about 12 one-line reverse-link edits and 3–4 generated images. Sourcing is concentrated in MYBA/CYBA, NOAA and HNMS. Blocked mainly on the gulet-season NEEDS MARK and the gulet photo.
Dependencies

Conflicts and how the page avoids them
(1) Parent /experiences/cruises/. Its 'Charter Brokerage' inclusion, FAQ 4 ('Is a private yacht charter complicated to arrange?') and FAQ 6 are the only existing charter content. Resolution: the parent keeps those short answers unchanged in substance and each gains a link here. The new page does not repeat FAQ 4's question and goes one level deeper (APA, contract, VAT, boat types, crew roles), per R10. It must not restate the parent intro's small-ship-versus-volume argument. (2) The sibling /experiences/cruises/mediterranean/ splits by ownership. Shared small-ship and cabin sailing (the Dalmatian Coast small ship and Greek Isles beyond Mykonos) lives there. Whole-boat crewed charter lives here. Each links to the other once with intent-specific anchor text. This page uses Mediterranean grounds only as charter cruising grounds, not as a small-ship itinerary guide. (3) Destination pages (Greece, Italy, France, Barbados & Eastern Caribbean, St. Barth's, Fiji) own their places and itineraries. This page summarises them in one exp-card each and links; it does not restate 'The Sailing Version' or 'Lau Group: Private Charter' copy. (4) Galápagos 'expedition yachts' are a licensed liveaboard product, so the page gives one clarifying sentence and a link, not a section. (5) Interim gulet link. /travel-journal/middle-east-destination-guide/ has no Turquoise Coast section and no ids, so the interim secondary link on parent event-card 4 is /travel-journal/mediterranean-october/ with no fragment. It moves to #gulets at launch. (6) Season contradiction. The parent's event-card says May–October, the middle-east guide's island-fact says June–September and the guide's line 291 says the season 'ends in October', and mediterranean-october says October is 'the last good month'. One reconciled season (NEEDS MARK) must be applied in the same PR to the new #gulets card, parent event-card 4's event-when and, if Mark changes it, the middle-east guide's island-fact. (7) No Turkey destination page, by design: one journal card cannot support it, and a Turkey page would be thin. The gulet intent is served by #gulets.
Hub, nav, llms.txt

The page is nested, the ItemList stays at 12, and the existing Cruises card already carries a 'Yacht Charters' tag inside an anchor card, so no link can be added there. public/llms.txt: no change. '12 trip types' counts depth-1 only, and the file has no per-experience list. Footer: no change; it keeps 'Cruises & Yachts' → /experiences/cruises/. Main nav: no change. Sections, in order
  1. intro-section. Label 'Why Charter'. First h2, e.g. 'The Whole Boat, Not a Cabin on It'. p.answer-capsule, then three intro-body paragraphs: (a) what a crewed charter is: you hire the vessel and its crew for a fixed term, usually a week, and the itinerary is agreed with the captain each morning within weather and cruising-ground limits; (b) who it is for: families and groups of friends filling every cabin, multigenerational trips (link /experiences/multigenerational-travel/), and celebrations (link /experiences/romance-celebration-travel/); (c) WHO IT IS NOT FOR, which NEW-CONTENT-PROMPT requires: a couple travelling alone, where the per-cabin economics favour a small ship (link /experiences/cruises/mediterranean/ once it exists, otherwise /experiences/cruises/), travellers who need a fixed daily port schedule, and anyone with severe motion sickness (this extends parent FAQ 5 and does not repeat it). The intro-image is a portrait np-/ni- crop that differs from the hero; see images.
  2. inclusions-section, used for the three boat types: 'Gulet, Catamaran or Motor Yacht' (h2). Three to four inclusion-items: Gulet (wooden motor-sailer from Turkey's Aegean and Mediterranean coast, a cabin count drawn from the gulet's own spec sheet, motors more than it sails, deck space for groups); Sailing or power catamaran (Greek islands and the Caribbean, stable at anchor, shallow draft for beach anchorages); Motor yacht (speed between islands, more range per day, higher fuel spend inside the APA); and optionally Crew (captain, chef, deckhand or stewardess, and what each does). Do not place a cabin, guest or crew number without a source, because charter guest limits vary by flag and by vessel (see sources).
  3. exp-cards-section id="destinations" (the standard experience id; also the target of the hero secondary CTA if it is switched). h2 e.g. 'Where We Charter'. exp-cards exp-cards--photo with six cards, each an <a class="exp-card"> to a destination page and never to /plan-your-trip/, with no <a> nested inside: Greek islands → /destinations/greece/#places; the Amalfi coast and the Italian islands → /destinations/italy/#places; French Riviera → /destinations/france/#places; Antigua and the Eastern Caribbean → /destinations/barbados-eastern-caribbean/#places; St. Barth's → /destinations/st-barths/; the Lau Group in Fiji → /destinations/fiji/#places. Turkey has no destination page, so it lives in section 4 (#gulets) and gets no card. Croatia also has no page; mention it only in body copy.
  4. events-section 'Trips We Plan Often' (h2). Three or four event-cards; every event-cta goes to /plan-your-trip/, which is the allowed event exception. Card 1 is <div class="event-card" id="gulets">, 'A Gulet of Your Own': 7 days, Turkey's Turquoise Coast, a Bodrum–Göcek or Fethiye routing, ruins by tender. Its event-when carries the reconciled season (NEEDS MARK). The id names this card in the 'Trips We Plan Often' events-section. It answers event-card 4 on its own and has at least two consumers (the parent event-card 4 secondary link, the romance page, and two journal posts). Card 2: Greek islands by crewed catamaran (Cyclades or Ionian, plus the July–August meltemi caveat). Card 3: a Caribbean motor yacht week (Antigua base, cross-linked in body to the Barbados & Eastern Caribbean 'The Sailing Version' itinerary rather than restating it). Optional card 4: Lau Group private charter of 7–10 days (summarised from /destinations/fiji/ with a link, not duplicated).
  5. A second inclusions-section, 'What We Handle on a Charter' (h2). This goes one level deeper than the parent's 'Charter Brokerage' item. Items: the broker and the vessel shortlist (association-member brokers, with sources); crew references and the pre-charter preference sheet; the contract (MYBA terms in the Med, and what the deposit and balance schedule says); the APA (how it is held, what it covers, accounting and refund at disembarkation); embarkation logistics (flights timed to a Saturday handover, marina transfers); and gratuity guidance, given as a sourced norm or left out.
  6. seasons-strip 'Charter Season by Ground' (h2). season-items: Eastern Mediterranean and Greece (with the meltemi caveat, sourced to HNMS); Turkey's gulet season (NEEDS MARK reconciliation, see below); the Caribbean (December–April, outside NOAA's 1 June–30 November Atlantic hurricane season), carrying season-item--best only if Mark confirms; the Fiji/Lau dry season of May–October, taken from /destinations/fiji/.
  7. exp-cta. h2 e.g. 'Plan a Charter Worth the Week'. Body asks for the coastline, the dates, the group size and the cabin split. Buttons: Plan My Charter → /plan-your-trip/ and a ghost link back to /experiences/cruises/ ('All cruise styles'), not to /experiences/.
  8. cost-range as a noBand row. A new key in tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs for the new content file, with unit e.g. 'Priced per vessel per week, not per person' and no currency amount. Includes: the boat, its crew, and the standard toys and fuel at a basic cruising allowance where the contract states it. Excludes: the APA (food, drink, fuel, berthing, which is drawn down and reconciled), VAT where it applies, crew gratuity, flights. Drivers must carry at least one checkable number: the APA percentage (from MYBA/CYBA/IYBA guidance), a country charter-VAT rate (from the Greek/Italian/Croatian tax authority), or NOAA hurricane dates. The source is a broker-association or tax-authority URL. No per-person band.
  9. page-faq with 8 questions using the pf-q/pf-a markup and ids pf-yacht-charters-1…8. Each answer opens with a complete answer in 25 words or fewer.
  10. read-more ('From the Journal'). Three cards: /travel-journal/mediterranean-october/ (Croatia and Turkey, the last gulet month), /travel-journal/middle-east-destination-guide/ (Turkey and the Turquoise Coast gulet season), /travel-journal/caribbean-mexico-destination-guide/ (charter operator to the cays). This meets the floor of at least two journal links.
FAQ candidates
  • What is the APA on a yacht charter, and do I get the unused part back?
  • What does the weekly charter fee include, and what is extra?
  • Gulet, catamaran or motor yacht: which suits our group?
  • How many people can a crewed charter carry? (answer with the regulatory guest limit from a sourced flag/MCA rule, not a guess)
  • How far ahead should a summer Mediterranean charter be booked? (NEEDS MARK for lead time)
  • When is gulet season on Turkey's Turquoise Coast? (the answer must use the reconciled season)
  • How much should we tip the crew? (only with a sourced industry norm; otherwise frame as 'set out in the broker's guidance')
  • Can the itinerary change once we are on board?
  • Is a charter good for a family with young children? (extends parent FAQ 6, does not repeat it; links /experiences/multigenerational-travel/)
  • Is VAT charged on a Mediterranean charter? (country-specific, sourced to the tax authority)
Authoritative sources to research
  • MYBA (Mediterranean Yacht Brokers Association), myba-association.com: the MYBA Charter Agreement, the APA definition, payment schedule and cancellation terms
  • CYBA International (Charter Yacht Brokers Association), cyba.net, and IYBA, iyba.org: APA practice, broker accreditation and the Caribbean contract norms
  • NOAA National Hurricane Center climatology, https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/climo/: Atlantic season 1 June–30 November (already cited on site in cost-ranges-data)
  • Hellenic National Meteorological Service (emy.gr): meltemi months for Aegean catamaran routing
  • Republic of Türkiye Ministry of Culture and Tourism / GoTürkiye (goturkiye.com): the Turquoise Coast, Bodrum, Fethiye and Göcek, and protected areas such as the Göcek SEPA (Special Environmental Protection Area)
  • Greek AADE (Independent Authority for Public Revenue) and the Italian Agenzia delle Entrate: VAT on yacht charters, if a VAT number is quoted
  • UK MCA Large Yacht Code / flag-state rules on the 12-passenger commercial limit: research before stating any guest cap
  • BVI National Parks Trust (bvinpt.org) mooring permits and Antigua Sailing Week (sailingweek.com, already linked on the Barbados page) for Caribbean regatta timing
  • Internal sources to reuse rather than restate: src/content-pages/travel-journal__mediterranean-october.html line 89 (22°C October sea, last gulet month); travel-journal__middle-east-destination-guide.html lines 250/255/291; destinations__barbados-eastern-caribbean.html lines 155–161; destinations__fiji.html lines 168–169

/experiences/cruises/european-river-cruises/ European River Cruises P2
Serves
CRU-T4 · CRU-E3
Primary intent
Commercial investigation. The visitor has already decided on a river cruise in Europe and wants to know which river (Rhine, Danube, Rhône or Douro) and which kind of ship fits them. They also want to know where the ships really dock (Vienna, Budapest, Lyon) and when to sail, with April and October as the shoulder months and low-water risk in late summer. Secondary: the Lyon–Avignon Rhône wine trip promised by the parent's event-card 3, deep-linked at #rhone.
Working title
European River Cruises | Rhine to Douro — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
Rhine, Danube, Rhône or Douro? River ships matched by kitchen and cabin, city-centre docking in Vienna, Budapest and Lyon, and April or October sailings. (153 chars)
H1
The H1 is the ExperienceLayout hero headline, and the page has only one. Unlike the parent's "The Right Ship Changes Everything", it must name the format and the choice. Direction: "Four European Rivers, Each With Its Right Ship". The words "European river" or "river cruise" must appear in the H1 itself, not only in the eyebrow. No <br> trick should split the keyword. The sub carries the decision in one sentence: the river sets the scenery, the ship sets dinner and the cabin, and the month sets the crowds. Avoid repeating the parent's "right ship" phrasing word for word.
Breadcrumb
Home > Experiences > Cruises & Yachts > European River Cruises (/ > /experiences/ > /experiences/cruises/ > /experiences/cruises/european-river-cruises/). The layout renders it from the new optional parent prop, and BreadcrumbList is generated from the same trail array, so the visible trail and the schema match.
Template
ExperienceLayout nested under /experiences/cruises/. Wrapper src/pages/experiences/cruises/european-river-cruises/index.astro (modelled on src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/index.astro) plus partial src/content-pages/experiences__cruises__european-river-cruises.html (or the repo's chosen nested-partial name). Props: name="European River Cruises", parent={ label: "Cruises & Yachts", href: "/experiences/cruises/" }. The label matches the parent's own name prop and breadcrumb leaf. Hero eyebrow is "Sea & River · Europe". ctaPrimary "Plan My River Cruise" goes to /plan-your-trip/. ctaSecondary "Compare the Rivers ↓" goes to #rivers. No pageCss. Slug decision: keep "european-river-cruises". The sibling decisions are /cruises/mediterranean/ (a place) and /cruises/yacht-charters/ (a format). This page is a format page, so the slug follows the format pattern of yacht-charters and matches the head query "european river cruises" exactly. The '-cruises' after /cruises/ repeats the parent, but the query match is worth it. Settle this together with S17 and do not rename after launch.
Schema
WebPage, BreadcrumbList with 4 ListItems, Service and FAQPage, all emitted by ExperienceLayout because schema-required keys on the /experiences/ prefix. Service: name "European River Cruise Planning", serviceType "River cruise planning", provider = the site Organization @id, areaServed = Germany, Austria, Hungary, France, Portugal and the Netherlands. List only countries the page actually covers. The four rivers go in the Service description, not in invented sub-nodes. FAQPage mirrors the 8 visible Q&As word for word. Its purpose is AI extraction, not chasing rich results. Do not add AggregateRating, Offer prices, TouristTrip, WebSite or SearchAction. Copy the og:image from Base.
Answer capsule
Place it directly under the first h2 (intro-title), 40–70 words, answering 'which river and when' in one pass. It should pair each river with its strength: Rhine for castles and the UNESCO Upper Middle Rhine gorge, Danube for Vienna and Budapest, Rhône for Lyon-to-Avignon wine country, Douro for terraced vineyards and high locks. Then state the typical length and the shoulder months. Allowed numbers, each tied to its source: (a) the Upper Middle Rhine Valley's 65 km length from UNESCO WHC list entry 1066; (b) a typical sailing of 7 nights, from at least two lines' published itinerary pages (Viking, AmaWaterways, Scenic, Uniworld or Avalon), stated as 'typically'; (c) the Carrapatelo lock lift on the Douro (about 35 m) from APDL (Douro waterway authority) only if verified there; (d) April and October as shoulder months, matching the parent tile and the seasons strip. Never invent passenger counts, ship dimensions, prices or 'most popular' rankings. If a figure cannot be sourced, cut it.
Word target
1,700–2,200 body words. Experience pages have no ceiling, but four river sections, inclusions, seasons and 8 FAQs need this much to be substantial without padding. Roughly 120–180 words per river card, about 80 per inclusion, and 50–90 per FAQ answer.
Effort
large
Dependencies
1) ExperienceLayout optional parent prop ({label, href}) built and merged first (shared with S17; Phase B), with the dist/ no-op diff proven and a 4-crumb fixture in tools/verify-checks.test.mjs. 2) Sibling slug convention agreed with S17 (yacht-charters/mediterranean) before the URL ships. 3) Tile map plus apply-tile-links tool, or a hand edit, for the parent's exp-card 4 and event-card 3 hrefs, landing in the same PR or after this page exists, so no link 404s. 4) Sourced hero (16:9 plus og- and hc- crops) for the Rhine, Danube or Rhône, entered via tools/image-manifest.mjs into images-b64/MANIFEST.json. Blocking. 5) A Rhône ev- crop to fix the parent's event-card 3 image. 6) New dated cost-range row in tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs with named line sources. 7) Mark's answers to the NEEDS MARK items, and verification of the Vienna mooring claim. 8) Reverse-link edits on the Europe hub, Portugal, /faq/, the parent and 2 journal posts, with France only on existing words because of the 3,500 ceiling. 9) The head-baseline update in its own commit; npm run build green, including journal-link floors and nestedCardAnchors; merge origin/main before the PR.
Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1) The parent /experiences/cruises/ keeps exp-card 4 and event-card 3 as summary tiles (one sentence each) and links down to this page. The parent's capsule, seasons and FAQ stay about cruising generally. This page owns all river detail. 2) The /destinations/europe/ FAQ pf-europe-5 answers the same core question. Keep its short answer and wrap 'A Danube or Rhine cruise' as the link, so the hub defers to this page. It names Scenic, Uniworld and Avalon; reuse those names here only after Mark confirms they are the lines he books. 3) /destinations/portugal/ owns the Douro land intent (Lisbon–Porto–Douro by train, quintas, harvest). This page treats the Douro only as a sailing and sends land intent to /destinations/portugal/#places. It does not compete on 'Douro Valley' head terms. 4) /destinations/france/ has no Rhône wine content (grep for Rhône/Hermitage/Châteauneuf returns nothing; the verifier's 'line 198' is Bordeaux). So the Rhône section links to France #places (Provence) and /experiences/food-wine-travel/ (Lyon), rather than to a non-existent Rhône section. France sits at 3,111 of 3,500 words, so any change there wraps existing words only. 5) The sibling /experiences/cruises/mediterranean/ (ocean small ships) and /yacht-charters/ split cleanly by format. Egypt's Nile cruise is out of scope (not European). 6) The /experiences/ hub card says 'a river cruise through Burgundy', which is barge/Saône country and no tile. Swap it for the Rhône or Douro. 7) FACT RISK: the tile claims ships dock 'in the center of Vienna'. Most river ships moor at Handelskai/Reichsbrücke, outside the Ringstrasse. Verify with the Port of Vienna and state the walk or shuttle honestly; Budapest and Lyon moorings are genuinely central. 8) First-hand claims on the parent ('producers we've worked with for years', 'clients who have just sailed them') must not be repeated until Mark confirms them. 9) The parent's IAATO-sourced cost band does not cover rivers; this page gets its own dated band.
Hub, nav, llms.txt
public/llms.txt: no count change. A nested /experiences/cruises/<slug>/ page is not counted by sectionCount, so '12 trip types' and the /experiences/ ItemList of 12 stay true. Optionally add a one-line entry under the cruises line if llms.txt lists sub-pages. Do not change the counts. Main nav and footer: unchanged. /experiences/ hub: retag or copy-fix the Cruises card (Burgundy → Rhône/Douro). Do not add a 13th card. /experiences/cruises/: exp-card 4 href, event-card 3 CTA to #rhone, intro-body link, an optional rivers row in the seasons strip, and an ev- image swap on event-card 3. /destinations/europe/ FAQ link, /destinations/portugal/ FAQ link, /faq/ link. Sitemap: the build adds the URL. tools/head-baseline.json: new entry in its own commit (node tools/verify-deployment.mjs --update-baseline). The parent's title and description are unchanged, so it needs no baseline update. src/data/tile-map.json records: cruises/exp-card/4 → this URL; cruises/event-card/3 → this URL with anchor 'rhone'.

FAQ candidates
  • Which European river is best for a first river cruise?
  • What is the real difference between a Rhine and a Danube cruise?
  • When is the best time of year for a European river cruise?
  • What happens if the river is too low or too high to sail?
  • Do river ships really dock in the centre of Vienna, Budapest and Lyon?
  • How is a Douro cruise different from the Rhine or Danube?
  • Should I take the ship's included excursions or book private guides?
  • How many nights do I need, and should I add land days before or after?
  • Are European river cruises suitable for travelers with limited mobility?
  • What does a European river cruise cost per person?
Authoritative sources to research
  • UNESCO World Heritage Centre: Upper Middle Rhine Valley (whc.unesco.org/en/list/1066), Wachau Cultural Landscape (/970), Budapest incl. the Banks of the Danube (/400), Historic Site of Lyon (/872), Historic Centre of Avignon (/228), Alto Douro Wine Region (/1046)
  • ELWIS / German Federal Waterways and Shipping Administration (WSV): Rhine gauge data (Kaub) for low-water risk and seasonality
  • ICPDR (International Commission for the Protection of the Danube River): Danube length and navigation facts
  • APDL (Administração dos Portos do Douro, Leixões e Viana do Castelo): Douro navigable waterway, the 5 locks and Carrapatelo lift
  • Compagnie Nationale du Rhône (CNR) and Voies navigables de France (VNF): Rhône locks and navigation between Lyon and Avignon
  • Port of Vienna (Wiener Hafen) / DDSG Blue Danube: cruise-ship mooring locations (Handelskai/Reichsbrücke), needed to verify the 'docked in the center of Vienna' claim
  • Inter Rhône (Rhône Valley wine interprofession): Hermitage and Châteauneuf-du-Pape appellations
  • Instituto dos Vinhos do Douro e do Porto (IVDP): Douro harvest timing
  • Cruise line fact sheets and published fare pages (Viking, AmaWaterways, Scenic, Uniworld, Avalon, Tauck): ship length, passenger capacity, cabin types, itinerary length and the dated cost band
  • CLIA (Cruise Lines International Association) State of the Cruise Industry report: river-cruise market context, if any market figure is used
  • EU travel/ETIAS official site and U.S. State Department country pages: entry requirements, if an FAQ touches documents
Inbound links (pages that must link here)

  • /experiences/cruises/ event-card 3 'The Rhône for Wine People': change the .event-cta href to /experiences/cruises/european-river-cruises/#rhone. Label direction: 'See the Rhône Trip'. Also swap its image (see imageGaps).
  • /experiences/cruises/ intro-body: wrap the existing words 'river ships that dock in the center of the city' as the link (descriptive anchor). /experiences/cruises/mediterranean-small-ship/ Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises P2
Serves
CRU-T1 · CRU-E1
Primary intent
Commercial-investigational: a traveller who wants a shared small-ship (roughly 50-300 guest) Mediterranean cruise and needs to know which coast (Dalmatia, Greek islands beyond Mykonos/Santorini, Amalfi/southern Italy), which ship size, which month (May-June or Sept-Oct) and how it differs from a megaship or a private charter. Not served by /experiences/cruises/ (one tile + FAQ 2), nor by /destinations/greece/ or /destinations/italy/ (land-based), nor by the yacht-charters child (private, whole-boat). No destination page covers Croatia at all.
Working title
Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises — Hit Your Mark Travel (55 characters incl. suffix; within 30-65)
Meta description
Small-ship Mediterranean cruises on Croatia's Dalmatian Coast, the quieter Greek islands and Amalfi, matched to ship size and May–June or Sept–Oct sailings. (156 characters; specific = the two shoulder windows. Within 110-165. Requires a head-baseline entry in its own commit.) (156 chars)
H1
Hero headline (the layout's H1) must carry "small ship" + "Mediterranean", e.g. along the lines of "The Mediterranean by Small Ship" — a two-line headline with <br> like the model page. The breadcrumb leaf / `name` prop = "Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises". Do not use "Mediterranean Cruises" alone and do not mention yachts in the H1 (keeps the S17/S23 split clean). Exactly one H1; first H2 is the intro-title.
Breadcrumb
Home > Experiences > Cruises & Yachts > Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises. Visible trail and BreadcrumbList must both render from the same `trail` array in ExperienceLayout (4 items; positions 1-3 with item URLs /, /experiences/, /experiences/cruises/; leaf without URL as the current layout does).
Template
ExperienceLayout, nested at src/pages/experiences/cruises/mediterranean-small-ship/index.astro with content partial src/content-pages/experiences__cruises__mediterranean-small-ship.html, wrapper modelled on src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/index.astro. Requires the NEW optional prop `parent?: { label: string; href: string }` on src/layouts/ExperienceLayout.astro (rules-data-template-qa.md s.11). It does not exist today: lines ~50-54 hard-code trail = [Home, Experiences, {name}] and Props has no parent field. Pass parent={{ label: "Cruises & Yachts", href: "/experiences/cruises/" }} (label must equal the parent page's `name` prop, "Cruises & Yachts"). schemaType="website". Hero: image /assets/img/jh-12-mediterranean-october-coast.jpg (has og crop og-jh-12-... in src/lib/og-crops), eyebrow "Small-Ship Cruising", ctaPrimary Plan My Cruise -> /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary to the routes section (#destinations, the id convention on every experience page). No --photo grid on any section that would need a Dalmatian photo until one exists.
Schema
Emitted by ExperienceLayout, nothing hand-written in the partial: WebPage (name "Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises", path /experiences/cruises/mediterranean-small-ship/) + BreadcrumbList (4 crumbs, matching visible trail) + Service (name/serviceType "Mediterranean Small-Ship Cruises", provider = Organization @id via lib/schema service()) + FAQPage (auto-extracted from page-faq by extractFaq; present for parity with experience pages, not chasing rich results). No AggregateRating, no Trip/Cruise/Offer markup with prices, no TouristTrip itinerary schema.
Answer capsule
40-70 words under the intro h2, answering 'Is a small-ship Mediterranean cruise worth it, and when?' directly: ship size decides the trip; small ships (give the guest-count range) dock in harbours like Hvar/Korčula and stay late where megaships tender and leave; the best windows are May-June and September-October; and anyone wanting the whole boat should see yacht charters (link). Numbers allowed and their sources: the May-June/Sept-Oct windows (already the parent page's stated windows; back with Croatian National Tourist Board / Greek national tourism season data); the guest-count range for 'small ship' must come from the actual line capacities of ships HYMT books (line fact sheets / deck plans) — NEEDS MARK to confirm which lines; any port-cap figure (e.g. Dubrovnik's daily cruise-ship/passenger limit) must come from the City of Dubrovnik / Dubrovnik Port Authority, and Santorini's from the Greek Ministry of Maritime Affairs announcements. Do not invent a megaship passenger figure — cite a named ship's published capacity or omit.
Word target
1,300-1,700 body words (experience-page standard 1,200-2,000; nested experience pages are under /experiences/ so the 3,500 destination ceiling does not apply, but keep it tight — the parent covers the general cruise argument). FAQ answers 60-200 words each, 7-8 questions.
Effort
large
Dependencies

Conflicts and how the page avoids them
(1) /experiences/cruises/yacht-charters/ (S23): both touch Greek islands and Croatia. Split: this page = shared small ships (cabins on a scheduled sailing, roughly 50-300 guests); S23 = whole-boat private charter (gulets, catamarans, motor yachts). Enforced by the qualified slug 'mediterranean-small-ship', 'small ship' in title/H1/name, no yachts in H1/title, an early outbound link to yacht charters, and rewriting the parent tile desc to drop 'and yacht'. (2) /experiences/cruises/ parent: keep its Med tile, Med season-item and FAQ 2 as short summaries that link down; do not duplicate the parent's inclusions/FAQ copy; move (not copy) the Dalmatian event-card detail here or keep the parent's as a teaser. (3) /destinations/greece/ and /destinations/italy/: land-based, hotel/guide-led pages; this page covers only the at-sea itinerary and ports-as-calls angle, links to their #places grids for land extensions, and does not repeat their Santorini/Amalfi property or town-choice advice. Greece has ~385 words headroom, Italy ~265 — no new copy there beyond a linked existing phrase. (4) /travel-journal/mediterranean-october/: the only Croatia content; this page links to it rather than restating its October-specific crowd argument. (5) Template: the parent prop on ExperienceLayout does not exist yet — the page cannot ship until it does, otherwise it emits a 3-crumb BreadcrumbList. (6) llms.txt '12 trip types' and the /experiences/ ItemList stay unchanged because the page is depth-2.
Hub, nav, llms.txt
/experiences/cruises/: exp-card 1 href -> new page and desc rewritten without 'and yacht' (via tile-map.json/apply-tile-links if Phase B landed); optional inline links in FAQ 2 and the Med season-item. /experiences/ hub: no new card (depth-2 page; ItemList stays 12); the Cruises card body could optionally name 'small-ship Mediterranean' but no required change. public/llms.txt: counts unchanged ('12 trip types' is depth-1); optionally add one indented line under Experiences for the new URL if llms.txt lists child pages — check the file format first and confirm the depth-1 count check ignores it. Footer: no change (Experiences column lists top-level pages only). Destination pages: Greece one linked existing phrase; Italy none (or FeaturedIn strip if approved).

Primary intent
Planning a trip to PLAY bucket-list golf: the Old Course ballot and the other St Andrews links, links trips in Scotland and Ireland, Pebble Beach, Royal Melbourne and the Sandbelt, and Casa de Campo. A secondary intent is attending The Open, including how to play and watch in the same week. Attending Augusta stays with /travel-journal/masters-field-report/. This page only points to it, in a section of 120 words or fewer.
Working title
Golf Travel: St Andrews to Pebble Beach — Hit Your Mark Travel
Meta description
The Old Course ballot is drawn two days ahead; Pebble Beach holds early tee times for resort guests. How a trip to play the great links, or watch The Open, works. (162 chars)
H1
The hero headline renders as the single h1, as on the parent. It names playing, not watching. Direction: "Golf Travel" as the eyebrow subject, with a headline along the lines of "Play the Courses You've Only Watched" (keep it under 70 characters). The hero sub names the concrete set: the Old Course, Pebble Beach, Royal Melbourne, Casa de Campo, and a week at The Open. It must not lead with Augusta, because that intent belongs to the Masters report.
Breadcrumb
Home > Experiences > Sports & Event Travel > Golf Travel (/ > /experiences/ > /experiences/sports-event-travel/ > /experiences/sports-event-travel/golf/). The layout renders it from the new parent prop, and BreadcrumbList is generated from the same trail array, so the two match exactly.
Template
ExperienceLayout, nested at src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/golf/index.astro, with content partial src/content-pages/experiences__sports-event-travel__golf.html. It is modelled on src/pages/experiences/sports-event-travel/index.astro: schemaType="website", name="Golf Travel", plus a hero object (image, eyebrow "Sports & Event Travel · Golf", headline, sub, ctaPrimary /plan-your-trip/, ctaSecondary "#destinations"). It also passes the NEW optional prop parent={{ label: "Sports & Event Travel", href: "/experiences/sports-event-travel/" }}. That prop does not exist yet: ExperienceLayout.astro has no parent prop today, and the change is shared with S47 (rules-data-template-qa.md section 11). The page uses only classes from experience.css and section-shared.css. No new CSS and no pageCss.
Schema
These are emitted by ExperienceLayout because the URL is under /experiences/: - WebPage - BreadcrumbList (4 items, including the parent) - Service: serviceType "Golf Travel"; provider references the Organization @id; areaServed may list Scotland, Ireland, United States, Australia and Dominican Republic only if the layout supports it, with no new schema code otherwise - FAQPage, built from the page-faq block Never add AggregateRating, HowTo, SportsEvent offers or ticket Offer markup. The page does not sell Open or Masters tickets.
Answer capsule
The capsule sits under the intro h2, is 40-70 words long, and answers 'How do you plan a trip to play the world's great golf courses?' directly. It must include at least one sourced number. Use the Old Course ballot lead time and/or its maximum handicap from the St Andrews Links Trust (standardgolf/ballot pages), or Pebble Beach's resort-guest advance tee-time window from Pebble Beach Resorts. Optionally add The Open's month or next venue from The R&A (theopen.com). Write 'as of <month year>' on the page beside any fee or policy number. No invented figures, and no claim that HYMT secures guaranteed tee times or private-club access.
Word target
1,400-1,900 body words (experience range 1,200-2,000). Experience pages have no 3,500 ceiling, but stay inside the range and do not pad. The Augusta pointer section is 120 words or fewer; each exp-card description is about 25-40 words; FAQ answers run 60-200 words each.
Effort
large

Conflicts and how the page avoids them
1. /travel-journal/masters-field-report/ owns 'attend the Masters / Augusta badges'. This page's Augusta section is 120 words or fewer, has no h3s, repeats no Amen Corner, Par 3 or badge-lottery detail, and links the report with anchor text like 'Masters patron badges'. The title, description and h1 do not contain 'Masters' or 'Augusta'. 2. /experiences/sports-event-travel/ (parent) keeps the broad 'sports event travel' query and its FAQ 6/7 golf answers. This page goes deeper, and the parent's FAQs link down rather than being duplicated. FAQ wording here must differ: the parent FAQ 7 is 'How do I choose between watching an event and playing it?', so this page asks the concrete 'Can I play a course the same week I watch The Open there?'. 3. /destinations/dominican-republic/ owns Casa de Campo, Teeth of the Dog and Punta Espada sequencing (itinerary, why-item and FAQ 5). This page gives Casa de Campo a 1-card or 1-paragraph summary and links there. It does not repeat the itinerary or the 'best golf in the DR' answer. 4. /destinations/uk-ireland/ has no golf content today, so St Andrews and links copy lives here. Keep it player-focused (ballot, handicaps, caddies); general Scotland touring stays on uk-ireland. 5. South Georgia's 'St Andrews Bay' (falklands-south-georgia, antarctica, polar-regions) is a name collision only. Never use falklands-south-georgia-st-andrews-bay.jpg here. 6. Interim state until this page ships: exp-card 2 should keep /plan-your-trip/ (with an exception record in the tile map) rather than point at the Masters report. The report serves about a third of the tile ('attend Augusta') and sends 'play St Andrews/Pebble' clicks to the wrong intent. If the plan insists on the report as the interim target, mark it short-lived in tile-map.json. 7. No query-parameter variants, and no separate /golf-travel/ depth-1 page, which would join the 12-item ItemList and break llms.txt.
Hub, nav, llms.txt

Do not add the page as a new depth-1 entry. - /experiences/ hub: no new card, and the ItemList stays at 12. Optionally add a 'Golf' exp-tag to the Sports & Event Travel card. - Footer: unchanged. Check the DR page's word headroom (3,433/3,500) and the australia page's (3,389/3,500). - images-b64/MANIFEST.json: append the new og and np crops through tools/image-manifest.mjs.
Sections, in order
  1. Hero (layout props): image e-25-pebble-beach-sunset.jpg, passed as preloadImage. This is the LCP image, so no lazy loading.
  2. intro-section: label 'Why Golf Travel'. The h2 states the answer, e.g. 'Most Great Courses Are Bookable, on Their Own Terms'. Then p.answer-capsule (40-70 words), 2-3 intro-body paragraphs on play vs. attend and on sequencing rounds around wind, rest days and a non-golfing partner. Then intro-image (portrait, see image gaps) with intro-image-label. intro-body links up to the parent experience page in body copy, as section 13 requires.
  3. 'Who this is for, and who it is not' (required by NEW-CONTENT-PROMPT section 3). This is a short h2 block in the intro-section idiom or the inclusions-section grid. Cover the handicap-certificate requirements (cite St Andrews Links Trust for the Old Course maximum handicap), walking-only and caddie norms on links courses (cite R&A or course sites), and the trip not suiting non-golfers unless a partner plan is built in. It answers the parent FAQ 'Is golf travel only for expert golfers?' at depth.
  4. exp-cards-section id="destinations" (keeps the parent's anchor convention for experience pages). Use exp-cards--photo ONLY if every card has a real photo; otherwise use plain exp-cards. One h2, e.g. 'Where We Plan Golf Trips'. Cards are anchors and have no inner <a>. (a) St Andrews and the Old Course ballot -> /destinations/uk-ireland/. (b) Links of Scotland and Ireland's west coast -> /destinations/uk-ireland/#places. (c) Pebble Beach and the Monterey Peninsula -> /destinations/north-america/. (d) Royal Melbourne and the Sandbelt -> /destinations/australia/#places. (e) Casa de Campo (Teeth of the Dog) and Punta Espada -> /destinations/dominican-republic/. (f) Augusta: attending the Masters -> /travel-journal/masters-field-report/.
  5. h2 section on St Andrews and the Old Course: ballot mechanics, advance tee times, handicap limit, the other Links Trust courses. Every number comes from the St Andrews Links Trust and is dated on the page. Uses intro-body paragraphs.
  6. h2 section on watching The Open: venue rotation (from The R&A), the practice-day vs. championship-day choice, and pairing a week at The Open with rounds on nearby links (expands the parent FAQ 7). It names the year and venue of the next Open from The R&A, with the date shown.
  7. h2 'The Masters' pointer section, 120 words or fewer, attend-only. Badge-access facts are summarised in at most two sentences, then a link to /travel-journal/masters-field-report/ with anchor text such as 'Masters patron badges and practice-round access'. No Augusta h3s and no repeat of the report's Amen Corner or Par 3 content.
  8. events-section 'Trips We Plan Often' (2-4 event-cards). Ideas: a St Andrews and East Lothian links week; Open week plus two rounds; Pebble Beach with Napa/Sonoma; Casa de Campo with the golf sequenced (summarised and linked to /destinations/dominican-republic/, not re-described). event-cta goes to /plan-your-trip/. Use ev- 3:4 images only where a real one exists.
  9. inclusions-section 'What HYMT Handles': tee-time and ballot strategy, caddie booking, transfers with club logistics, and a rest-day and partner plan. No unverifiable 'private club access' claim without NEEDS MARK.
  10. seasons-strip 'When to Play Where': Scotland/Ireland May-Sept, Melbourne's southern summer, Caribbean Dec-Apr (matches the DR itinerary), and The Open in July. Each window is sourced from the destination page it echoes or from the authority.
  11. exp-cta, mirroring the parent's copy pattern ('Tell me which course...').
  12. cost-range: a new data row in tools/cost-ranges-data.mjs keyed 'experiences__sports-event-travel__golf.html'. Drivers: green fees (published visitor fees from St Andrews Links Trust and Pebble Beach Resorts, dated); the resort-stay requirement for advance Pebble tee times; caddie fees. A source line links the authority.
  13. page-faq: 6-10 items using the pf-item / pf-q-h / button.pf-q / pf-a role=region pattern with ids pf-golf-N. The first sentence of each answer is 25 words or fewer.
  14. read-more: at least 2 journal links. Use masters-field-report, caribbean-mexico-destination-guide (Amanera's private course; Casa de Campo context) and europe-destination-guide (Scotland), or south-pacific-destination-guide (Cape Kidnappers/Laucala) instead of the europe guide. Do not use kentucky-derby-field-report: it is not about golf.
FAQ candidates
  • How do I get a tee time on the Old Course at St Andrews?
  • What handicap do I need to play the Old Course and other championship links?
  • Do I have to stay at The Lodge at Pebble Beach to play Pebble Beach Golf Links?
  • Can I play a course the same week I watch The Open there?
  • How many rounds should a golf trip have, and do I need rest days?
  • Is golf travel worthwhile if my partner doesn't play? (answer with a non-golfer plan; links to /destinations/uk-ireland/ and /destinations/dominican-republic/)
  • When is the best time to play links golf in Scotland and Ireland?
  • Can visitors play Royal Melbourne and the other Sandbelt courses?
  • Do I need caddies, and are carts allowed on links courses?
  • Can you get me Masters badges? (attend intent, at most 2 sentences, then a link to /travel-journal/masters-field-report/)
Authoritative sources to research
  • St Andrews Links Trust (standrews.com): Old Course ballot, advance tee times, handicap limits, published visitor green fees, other Links courses
  • The R&A / The Open (randa.org, theopen.com): Open venue rotation, dates, practice-day and ticket categories
  • Pebble Beach Resorts (pebblebeach.com): resort-guest vs. non-guest tee-time windows, published green fee, caddie policy
  • Royal Melbourne Golf Club (royalmelbourne.com.au): visitor/international guest-play policy and letter-of-introduction requirement
  • Casa de Campo (casadecampo.com.do): already cited on /destinations/dominican-republic/; reuse it, do not re-cite different figures
  • PGA Tour (pgatour.com): already cited on the DR page for the Corales event
  • Augusta National / masters.com: only if the Augusta pointer states a fact, preferably taken from the field report
  • Golf Ireland / Scottish Golf (scottishgolf.org): handicap certificate guidance for visiting players

10Combination, ambiguous and rename decisions

How the known disagreements were settled

DisagreementDecision
St. Lucia: new page vs the Eastern Caribbean page's #placesNew /destinations/st-lucia/. Until it ships, the All-Inclusive tile links the Barbados & Eastern Caribbean page's Soufrière & the Pitons card. The Beach tile is renamed "St. Lucia" (Anguilla has no page and no image). At launch, that Barbados card's href also moves to the new page, which is the strongest consolidation signal.
Disney/Universal: /destinations/orlando/ vs /destinations/disneyland/Both are real, different places. Orlando serves the Disney World and Universal tiles (Universal deep-links a dated "Disney or Universal?" section). Disneyland is owner-gated, and the default is to drop the tile unless six honest photographed cards can be produced.
Golf and Horse Racing to journal posts; Tennis and F1 to new pagesTennis, F1 and Golf get detail pages, each a distinct trip type with commercial demand and nothing on the site answering it. Horse Racing stays on the Derby report with a new #other-meetings section. The Masters card keeps linking the Masters report, which is that subject's canonical page.

The tile is renamed "Heli-Skiing" (eyebrow "British Columbia") and links the heli-ski field report. The Verbier/Zermatt chalet line is cut unless an Alps page is commissioned.
Willamette Valley to a journal postKept, as the canonical target rather than a placeholder. The winery-route post is the site's full answer, and a destination page would compete with it. Trim the tile's truffle and Dungeness crab clause, or add 40 cited words on each to the post.
Mexico under five experiences with three targetsThe coastal tiles (All-Inclusive, Family, Wellness) all go to /destinations/riviera-maya-los-cabos/, and Multigenerational goes to the Caribbean & Mexico hub's villa itineraries. Interior cultural Mexico (Culture) is an owner decision: Oaxaca & Mexico City page, or rename the tile "Yucatán & Mérida". No /destinations/mexico/ page, which would split equity with the coastal page.
