Trips We Plan Often (event cards): one policy for all 48

One rule for all 48 'Trips We Plan Often' event-cards (4 per experience page x 12).

(1) PRIMARY CTA never moves. .event-cta keeps its label ('Plan This Trip' / 'Request X Package') and its target, /plan-your-trip/. Where the experience maps cleanly to a supported form type, append the prefill: beach-island-escapes and all-inclusive-vacations -> ?type=beach; family-travel -> ?type=family; multigenerational-travel -> ?type=group; cruises -> ?type=cruise; safari-wildlife-travel -> ?type=safari; romance 'The Honeymoon, Full Stop' -> ?type=honeymoon; romance 'The Birthday Worth the Flight' -> ?type=group. Every other card (adventure, culture, food-wine, sports, wellness, and the romance anniversary/proposal cards) keeps the bare URL. The form's canonical strips the query, so this creates no duplicate URLs. Verify the form actually reads ?type= before shipping.

(2) Each card may carry AT MOST ONE secondary text link, placed in .event-body under the CTA (the .event-card is a div; nestedCardAnchors guards only .exp-card and .place-card, so confirm that selector before the first one ships). It is styled by one shared class in experience.css (no pageCss) and phrased as '<Place> in detail →'. Its target is always the SAME canonical page as the card's subject (see subjects), choosing the deepest stable section that honestly matches, in this order: #itineraries when that page's Trips We Plan Often section carries the same or a closely matching itinerary; else #places when a place-card is the closest match; else a named section id on an approved new page; else the page root.

(3) A secondary link ships only when the target text already supports the card. If an enhancement is pending (Inca Trail -> Peru, Riviera Maya Family Week, Portugal Three-Generation, Burgundy -> France, Sri Lanka Ayurveda, Japan onsen towns) or a new page is pending (Dolomites, St. Lucia, tennis #wimbledon, formula-1 #monaco, mediterranean #dalmatian-coast, european-river-cruises #rhone, yacht-charters #gulets), the card ships with no secondary link, or with the named interim, until the target exists.

(4) Card copy that contradicts its target page is fixed before the link goes live: Seychelles flights/Beau Vallon/season, St. Barth's season, Sri Lanka season and the 'under three hours' claim, Bali May-October, Tulum November-April, Japan Sunrise Seto routing and Beppu/Dogo claims, Peru acclimatization (Sacred Valley first), Patagonia dates, Cape Town 'guaranteed Big Five' and season, Costa Rica beach half, and renaming the 'Riviera Maya Adults Escape' collision.

(5) Never mark event-cards up as Offer, TouristTrip or Event.

(6) Anchor convention: id=\"itineraries\" on section.itineraries-section in ONE pass across every destination partial that has the section (Maldives has none; use the page root). This rejects id=\"trips\". Ship it with a card-fragment-resolves verifier check plus fixtures that go red, because the dead-link check strips fragments and would pass a missing id.

Exceptions: the romance anniversary, proposal and birthday cards get no secondary link (the occasion is the product, and any destination would pre-empt it). The Masters card links the existing journal explainer (/travel-journal/masters-field-report/) because that is the subject's canonical. Alpine Ski Season links the heli-ski report labelled for the heli option only. Antarctica, Done Properly links /destinations/antarctica/#itineraries, not the polar hub. The Maldives Overwater Classic links the Maldives page root (no itineraries section). Multigenerational Italy (family-travel) links /destinations/italy/#itineraries rather than the multigenerational experience page. Jamaica Couples Week, Tulum Jungle Digital Detox, Sri Lanka Ayurvedic Immersion and Japan Onsen & Ryokan Circuit use #places because no matching itinerary exists.

Verified The pre-fill reads ?type= and matches it to a real checkbox; the six supported values are beach, cruise, family, group, honeymoon and safari. Recommendation One review change: the honeymoon card gets no secondary link either, so all three occasion cards follow the same "don't pre-empt the destination" rule.

Recommendations merged or dropped, and why

  • St. Lucia SPLIT to /destinations/barbados-eastern-caribbean/#places (beach auditor): lost to CREATE /destinations/st-lucia/. Barbados is fit 2 with 184 words of headroom and 0 all-inclusive mentions. It is kept only as the interim target.
  • Anguilla (beach tile 6) and any /destinations/anguilla/: dropped. No page, no image, no content.
  • /destinations/universal-orlando/ and /destinations/walt-disney-world/: merged into /destinations/orlando/.
  • Disneyland folded into Orlando: rejected (wrong place and breadcrumb). Kept as a separate conditional subject.
  • Family event-card 4 secondary link to /experiences/multigenerational-travel/: replaced with /destinations/italy/#itineraries (one canonical per subject). The two experience pages cross-link in body copy instead.
  • Maldives event-card secondary link to /travel-journal/maldives-overwater-vs-beach-villa/: replaced with the Maldives page root. The post moves into the beach page's Read More.
  • Anchor id="trips" (adventure and food-wine auditors): merged into the sitewide id="itineraries".
  • Italy 'Emilia-Romagna & Piedmont' place-card (food-wine): dropped. No 16:9 photo, it would stale 'Six Regions', and it does not fit the 265-word headroom. Replaced by a ~30-word Langhe clause in the existing 'Italy Food & Wine' itinerary.
  • Italy: separate family FAQ, multigenerational FAQ and romance Amalfi FAQ merged into one family/three-generation FAQ plus a <=20-word honeymoon clause in Amalfi FAQ 4. The culture Sicily/Noto addition is dropped.
  • Greece: four separate FAQs (all-inclusive, Thessaloniki, villa, Santorini honeymoon) merged into two. The all-inclusive season-strip sentence and the 'Resort Coast' tag are dropped.
  • Japan: the culture auditor's optional Nishiki/Gion sentence is dropped. The multigenerational, romance, wellness and food-wine requests are merged into one spec.
  • Riviera Maya multigenerational villa note: not added unless Mark confirms he sells butler villa resorts (NEEDS MARK). The multigenerational tile goes to the Caribbean & Mexico hub instead.
  • Caribbean hub: the family FAQ (~110 words) and multigenerational FAQ (~80) merged into one ~120-word FAQ.
  • Jamaica new FAQ 9 (~120 words): reduced to <=60 words folded into FAQ 5 (147 words of headroom).
  • Sri Lanka Galle place-card description addition (~30 words): dropped (162 words of headroom). The FAQ plus a tag is the short module.
  • Costa Rica adult-adventure itin-card: dropped (148 words of headroom).
  • France: the 'do the place-card first' sequencing is reversed. The Burgundy itinerary ships first (its portrait exists) and the place-card waits for a 16:9 photo. Duplicating the hub's Burgundy itinerary is rejected in favour of move-and-summarise.

  • Croatia and Turkey destination pages: rejected. The Dalmatian and gulet sections on the new cruise sub-pages serve the cards.

11Template and data architecture

Templates

Recommendation

  • Existing destination pages: DestinationLayout unchanged for routing, plus one optional generated module, "Featured in".
  • New destination pages: DestinationLayout with a region prop. Model page: destinations__turks-caicos.html.
  • New experience-detail pages: ExperienceLayout with one new optional prop, parent?: { name: string; path: string }, which mirrors DestinationLayout's region. With it, the visible trail and the BreadcrumbList both read Home › Experiences › Sports & Event Travel › Tennis. Without it, output is byte-identical to today. The verifier already requires ExperienceLayout recursively under src/pages/experiences/; schema-required keys on the prefix, so Service schema is emitted and required; and the depth-1 count keeps "12 trip types" true.
  • Section mix for a detail page, existing classes only: intro-section with answer capsule → exp-cards-section (venues or events; swatch grid until every card has a real photo) → events-section → inclusions-section → seasons-strip (the calendar) → exp-cta → cost-range (a row in cost-ranges-data.mjs) → page-faq → read-more (≥2 journal links). No new CSS beyond the two classes below.
  • Rejected: a new ExperienceDetailLayout, which would duplicate ~120 lines and a stylesheet for one crumb. DestinationLayout for thematic pages: wrong schema and the stat rail. JournalLayout: Article schema and a dated byline on an evergreen commercial page. Flat /experiences/formula-1-travel/: it joins the hub ItemList, breaks the llms.txt count and loses the parent crumb.

Data model: map now, component later

Recommendation src/data/tiles.mjs, plain ESM so both Astro and the node tools can import it. It is keyed by partial filename, like COST_RANGES, with rows in document order:

export const TILES = {
  'experiences__beach-island-escapes.html': [
    { kind: 'exp-card', name: 'Maldives', href: '/destinations/maldives/',
      subject: 'S13', status: 'live', note: 'country page' },
    { kind: 'exp-card', name: 'St. Lucia', href: '/destinations/barbados-eastern-caribbean/#places',
      final: '/destinations/st-lucia/', status: 'interim', note: 'swap at St. Lucia launch' },
    { kind: 'event-card', name: 'Maldives Overwater Classic', cta: '/plan-your-trip/?type=beach',
      more: '/destinations/maldives/', exception: 'itinerary teaser: CTA stays on the form' },
    // …
  ],
}
export const featuredIn = (path) => /* experience pages whose rows target this pathname */
  • tools/tile-links-apply.mjs, idempotent and modelled on p3-8-cost-insert.mjs. It pairs each <a class="exp-card" href> with the next exp-card__name, refuses on any count or name mismatch, rewrites only the href (and the event-card CTA and its secondary link), and prints what it changed. --check exits non-zero on drift.
  • src/components/FeaturedIn.astro, rendered by DestinationLayout from featuredIn(Astro.url.pathname). It emits nothing when the list is empty. It renders as a .featured-in strip ("Featured in: Beach & Island Escapes · Romance & Celebration Travel"), at most 40 words, before the read-more section. bodyWords strips it, so it never counts against the ceiling. Reverse links are derived from the map, so they cannot rot.
  • Verifier checks in tools/content-checks.mjs and verify-deployment.mjs, each with fixtures in verify-checks.test.mjs that prove it goes red: exp-card-targets (no card links the form, an in-page fragment, an external URL or a query variant unless its map row says exception); card-fragment-resolves (every #id on a card or secondary link exists on the target page); tile-map-parity (built href equals map; no map row for a missing card; no card without a row); featured-in-parity (every targeted destination renders a link back).
  • Why not move the cards into a component now: the 78 cards hold hand-tuned copy and the photo gate. Moving them touches all 12 pages, the intake tool and the photo check at once for no visible gain. Revisit once the map has settled, then extend it to the 330 .place-cards.

12SEO/AIO and duplicate-content strategy

  • One canonical per subject. 56 subjects, 56 URLs. Multiple tiles share a target (Japan ×5, Italy ×6, Greece ×4); the tiles carry the experience angle, and the destination page stays about the destination. Canonicals are self-referencing and inherited from Base.astro; nothing here overrides them.
  • No style-variant URLs. No /destinations/maldives-honeymoon/, no query variants, no per-experience sections on destination pages (R6).
  • Parent and child never compete. A nested page owns the specific query ("Wimbledon debenture tickets"), and the parent keeps the broad one ("sports event travel"). The parent's Monaco cost drivers and capsule clause move to the F1 page, leaving one clause on the parent that links down. The Masters report keeps the Augusta query; the golf page caps Augusta at ~120 words and links it.
  • Hub vs new page. At launch, the Barbados & Eastern Caribbean card for Soufrière and the Europe hub's river FAQ link down to the new pages, so the hubs defer to the new canonicals.
  • Schema per page type. Destination: WebPage + BreadcrumbList + TouristDestination + FAQPage. Experience detail: WebPage + BreadcrumbList (4 items) + Service + FAQPage. Journal: Article + BreadcrumbList. Never Offer, TouristTrip or Event on itinerary cards, and never AggregateRating.
  • AIO on every new page and every enhanced FAQ. A 40–70 word answer capsule under the first h2 with at least one number from a named source. FAQ first sentences answer in ≤25 words. Time-sensitive facts carry a visible "Checked <Month> 2026" date: Epic Universe, Typhoon Lagoon, gulet season, the Grand Slam ballots, the F1 calendar.
  • Titles and descriptions for new pages are drafted in each brief within 30–65 and 110–165 characters. Each new page's head-baseline entry is its own commit. No reuse in this plan changes an existing title or description.

13Internal-linking and breadcrumb strategy

  • Forward: tile → canonical page, generated from the map. Intro, seasons and FAQ copy on the experience page that names a tile subject links it once per section with descriptive anchor text ("Ayurvedic programmes in Sri Lanka"), never "Explore →" and never inside a card.
  • Reverse: the generated "Featured in" strip on every targeted destination page, plus one prose link from each enhanced page's new FAQ or itinerary, never inside a .place-card. On pages under 200 words of headroom, the link is wrapped on existing words.
  • Sideways: one body-copy link each way between Family and Multigenerational (they sell the same Italy and Hawaii trips). Each new detail page links up to its parent in body copy and across to the 2–4 destination pages it relies on.
  • Journal floor: unchanged (≥2 journal links per destination or experience page, ≥2 destination or experience links per post). Swap /travel-journal/maldives-overwater-vs-beach-villa/ into Beach & Island's Read More; it already links there and is not linked back.
  • Breadcrumbs never vary by referrer. A visitor from Romance lands on Home › Destinations › Asia › The Maldives; "Featured in" is the way back. Nested pages: Home › Experiences › Cruises › Private Yacht Charters.
  • Hubs: retag the /experiences/ hub cards to name the subjects each page's tiles actually carry (body copy, no head-baseline change). Make the destination hub headings count-free ("Destinations worth planning properly") in the same PR as the first new card, so a seventh card cannot go stale. Remove the stray "Anguilla" tag from destinations.html.

Cross-page findings to fix along the way

  • PRECEDENT: all 78 exp-cards and all 48 event-ctas currently point at /plan-your-trip/. This plan is the first to route exp-cards to content. Ship through a central map (architecture-read dataModelOptions B: src/data/tiles.mjs plus an idempotent inserter), together with exp-card-targets, card-fragment-resolves, tile-map-parity and featured-in-parity verifier checks, each with fixtures that go red.
  • REVERSE LINKS: only 3 of 68 destination pages link to any experience page (costa-rica, dominican-republic, zambia-victoria-falls). Every enhanced or reused target needs one inline link back in prose, a FAQ answer or an itin-desc, never inside a .place-card. On pages under 200 words of headroom (Seychelles 80, DR 67, Morocco 126, Argentina 141, Jamaica 147, Costa Rica 148, Sri Lanka 162, Barbados 184, South Africa 191, Australia 111) the link is wrapped on existing words.
  • STALE HEADING COUNTS on experience pages: adventure 'Six Destinations Worth Moving Through' (Dolomites rename keeps six; adding ski/dive tiles would stale it); all-inclusive 'Six Destinations With Properties Worth Booking'; beach 'Six Destinations We Know in Detail' (holds after the St. Lucia rename); culture 'Six Destinations With Layers Worth Finding' (holds unless Bhutan/Oman are added); family 'Nine Destinations We Know for Families', 'Three of the nine are theme-park resorts' and the dated September 2026 note (all change if Disneyland is dropped); food-wine 'Nine Destinations Where the Food Explains the Place' and the capsule '7 regions across 3 harvest calendars'; multigenerational 'Six Destinations Built for the Whole Family'; safari 'Six Countries We Know Inside Out' (holds under either Namibia path); sports cost basis 'six sports' (changes if Football & Rugby is removed).

14Content and image requirements

Content

  • Copy fixes before links (P1, small): the malaria claim on the Family Safari tile, the Africa hub (4 statements) and the africa-destination-guide post, cited to the CDC; plus the season and fact corrections listed in §10 and §13.
  • Enhancements: 18 pages, ~30–250 words each, totals in §7. All FAQ additions follow the accordion rules (button in heading, aria-controls, role="region", answer visible without JS).
  • New pages: 1,300–3,000 body words each, per brief. Every new page carries 6–10 real FAQs, ≥2 journal links, and cited sources from the brief's list.
  • NEEDS MARK: each brief lists 7–10 first-hand facts only Mark can supply (packages sold, properties used, prices, personal experience). None may be invented, and a page does not ship with one unresolved.
  • Journal gaps to commission later (each changes the "32 field reports" count): trekking, cruise, tennis and F1 field reports.

Images

Verified against the 1,194-file listing (public/assets/img/ is gitignored; run npm run restore before checking on disk).

  • Reuse targets: all 78 tiles already have a real photo, so href changes do not touch the photo gate. Every new hero needs an og- 1200×630 crop via tools/make-og-crops.mjs, and every new image enters MANIFEST.json through tools/image-manifest.mjs. A card grid stays a non-photo swatch grid until every card has a real photo.

17QA and acceptance checklist

Site-wide

  • grep -c 'class="exp-card" href="/plan-your-trip/"' src/content-pages/experiences__*.html returns 0 for every file except tiles whose page is pending, and each of those has a map row marked interim or exception.
  • node tools/tile-links-apply.mjs --check reports zero drift; the map has 126 rows and no duplicates.
  • npm run build is green with exp-card-targets, card-fragment-resolves, tile-map-parity and featured-in-parity; verify-checks.test.mjs has a red fixture for each.
  • Every targeted destination page renders a "Featured in" link back to each experience that targets it; the 68 − n untargeted pages are byte-identical.
  • No edited country page is over 3,400 body words (verifier bodyWords).
  • Every ?type= pre-fill ticks the right checkbox on /plan-your-trip/ (manual check for each of the 6 values).

Each new page

  • Title 30–65 characters; description 110–165 with a specific; one h1; no skipped heading levels.
  • Answer capsule of 40–70 words under the first h2, with a sourced number; 6–10 FAQs, first sentences ≤25 words; accordion markup per the house rules.
  • Breadcrumb visible and in BreadcrumbList (4 items for nested pages); page-type schema per §12.
  • ≥2 journal links, ≥2 descriptive internal links, the up-link to the parent, and reverse links from every page listed in the brief's "inbound links".
  • Hero passed as preloadImage; og- crop; every img has alt, width, height, decoding="async"; lazy below the fold only.
  • In dist/sitemap-0.xml and tools/head-baseline.json; llms.txt count correct; zero unresolved NEEDS MARK comments.

Visual and accessibility

  • Screenshots at 375, 768 and 1280 px of one changed experience page, one "Featured in" destination page and one new detail page.
  • Keyboard tab order through the tiles, the event-card secondary links and the "Featured in" chips; FAQ answers visible with JavaScript off.
  • npm run verify:prod is green about 10 minutes after each merge.
